INTRODUCTION
Nights of Chrome is a role-playing game system, with 
D20 mechanics, set in a dystopian, cyberpunk future, 
and heavily influenced by the Cyberpunk Vieja Escuela 
manual. Honestly, the basis of Nights of Chrome takes 
a lot of common ground with Cyberpunk Vieja Escuela, 
being more of an update and expansion, rather than 
something completely new. The motive, in essence, is to 
give some more depth, options and possibilities to the 
rules of the game, but without going overboard, trying 
to keep the simplicity and immediacy of the original 
system, and leaving the things that work well as they 
are. The system is what it is, and if you liked and enjoyed 
the Cyberpunk Vieja Escuela system, you might like this 
manual.
The manual will be focused exclusively on rules, 
equipment, weaponry, implants and enemies. There is 
no setting, history, context, opposing factions, or events 
of any kind, giving complete freedom to the Game 
Director to place the action and story where he/she 
deems appropriate or best suits the needs of his/her 
game.
Note that these rules, if the Game Director and the 
Players consider it appropriate, can modify, alter or 
change them if they wish. Nothing in this manual is set 
in stone, so don't be afraid to change it if you think it will 
improve the game or make it more fun.
WHAT IS A ROLE-PLAYING GAME?
I suppose that all those who are going to read this 
manual already have some basic notions of what a 
role-playing game is, but it does not hurt to remember 
the basic concepts (in a very summarized form) for the 
newcomers.
A role-playing game, in essence, is a game in which one 
or more players play a certain role, role or personality. 
When a player plays the role of X it means that they 
are playing the role of a Player Character (PC for short). 
The game is directed, narrated and refereed by a Game 
Director (GD for short), who will set out the adventure, 
present the challenges, traps and events, and control 
the Non-Player Characters (NPCs), or enemy characters 
during combat. 
As the PC overcome the different challenges and 
combats, whether exploring dungeons, castles, forests, 
wastelands, deserts, etc., they will acquire new 
equipment (which can be better or worse) and will 
be able to acquire experience points to improve their 
character's stats. The adventures may vary in their 
development and contain any type of event (exploration, 
infiltration, combat, social mechanics, research, etc.), 
the main objective being the interaction between PCs 
or GD, the collaboration between PCs, and at the end of 
the day, to have a fun and entertaining time.
ABOUT THE USE OF DICE
Whenever an action is to be performed, which may 
be significant during the adventure, which may be 
dangerous, or which involves some challenge on the 
part of the PCs, a dice roll must be made to see if it is 
successful. If the difficulty assigned by the GD to that 
action is exceeded, the PCs will succeed with his action. 
If the result of the dice is lower than the difficulty, the 
PC will have failed the roll, and may suffer some penalty 
(not achieving the desired objective or his character will 
suffer some kind of damage, for example).
How do you read the dice in this manual? If an 
abbreviation indicates, for example, "1D6", it means that 
you must roll a six-sided die. The first number indicates 
how many dice to roll, and the second number indicates 
the number of faces that die will have: a "1D6" will be 
one six-sided die; a "2D8" will be two eight-sided dice; 
"3D12" will be three twelve-sided dice, and so on.
If, for example, the abbreviation is "1D6+2", it means 
that you must roll a six-sided die, and add a two to the 
result. While the first number indicates how many dice 
to roll, and the second the number of faces of those dice, 
10


the third number will indicate the value to be added to 
the result obtained by the dice
ABOUT THE CYBERPUNK 
CONTEXT
To provide some context, the term Cyberpunk belongs 
to a literary genre that emerged during the eighties (last 
century), promoted by the authors Bruce Stearling, 
William Gibson, John Shirley and Neal Stephenson 
(among others), where a dark future is represented, more 
or less near, dystopian, where large corporations have 
reached great heights of power (being able to influence 
considerably on governments), and have achieved great 
technological advances, in exchange for a worsening of 
the living conditions of the citizens. The moral of the 
characters is diffuse, having to face internal and social 
conflicts that are difficult to resolve. The motto “Hight 
Tech, Low Life” is the maximum representation of the 
socioeconomic context within the cyberpunk stories.
The plots, broadly speaking, revolve around corporate 
warfare, industrial espionage, gang warfare, media 
control, network espionage, Artificial Intelligence (AI) 
uprisings, settling of scores, etc.
Technological implants (or rather cyber-implants), and 
incursions into cyberspace (the hypothetical "Internet" 
of a near future during the eighties) to hack or steal from 
corporate databases, are the usual tone in Cyberpunk 
themed stories. Stories, populated by characters with 
nothing to lose and much to gain, outcasts of society, 
and, usually, uprooted from any nation or social status: 
solos, hackers, hired killers, ronins, prostitutes, private 
detectives, gang members, etc.
As for cyberpunk in role-playing games, the references 
are clear: Cyberpunk 2020 and Shadowrun. The first 
is more earthly, more realistic and attached to classic 
literary references; the second introduces fantastic 
elements such as orcs, elves and other races typical of 
role-playing games.

CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
J
ack's bar, a slum in the 
lower part of town, was 
Specially busy. Normally it 
was a dull, dreary place with 
few customers, but that night 
the Edge Rippers were playing at 
a concert hall down the street, 
so Jack's became a meeting place 
for the band's fans. Old Jack and 
his sons, war veterans, couldn't 
keep up with all the fans of the 
band that crowded in front of 
the grimy counter of their bar. 
Beers, shots and mixers were 
being dispensed at lightning 
speed, and Edge Rippers fans were 
constantly visiting the bar's 
only bathroom.
And that hustle and bustle, that 
crowd of people, was something 
that Agent Dover hated. Dressed 
discreetly, and sheltered behind 
a narrow table at the back of the 
room, Alex Dover was waiting for a 
team to arrive for a preliminary 
interview. He had been hired 
by the Wired Group, a company 
dedicated to the search and 
exploitation of rare earths, to 
steal confidential documentation 
from a government office, and 
Alex wanted to assemble a group 
of candidates to carry out the 
job.
"I just hope they dress better 
than these idiots!" thought Alex 
with distaste, before taking a 
drink from the glass in front of 
him.
Which didn't happen.
One of the Edge Rippers' fans, 
who was involved in a fight, 
was violently pushed and fell  
 
13


CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
on 
Alex's 
table. 
The 
blow 
knocked Alex off balance, and 
the glass fell to the ground. 
Surprised, Alex got up angrily, 
grabbed the band's fan and shook 
him violently.
	
—You fucking kid! You threw 
the glass at me, you piece of...!
Before 
he 
could 
finish 
his 
sentence, a dozen fanatics, pumped 
full of drugs and covered with 
second-hand implants, surrounded 
Agent Dover and brandished their 
knives and machetes.
	
—What the fuck are you on about, 
man? —said one Edge Rippers fan.
	
—You want us to cut your throat 
and line our cocks with your 
hide, don't you? —said another 
fan with a defective eye implant.
	
—Calm down, calm down, I don't 
want any trouble, okay? —shouted 
Alex with his hands raised. His 
eyes fell on his suitcase, which 
rested on a chair under the 
table, and he thought about the 
automatic pistol inside. "Will 
it give me time to get it before 
these disturbed people cut me 
open?"
Alex took a small step toward 
the chair where the briefcase 
rested... something that did 
not go unnoticed by the Edge 
Rippers' fanatics, who gritted 
their teeth and jumped on him. 
And right after that, there was 
an explosion of violence. Tracer 
bullets, lasers and smoke flooded 
Jack's bar. Severed limbs flew 
through the air and hemoglobin 
eruptions painted the walls. 
Alex instinctively jumped to 
the side, hid under the table and 
covered his head. He completely 
forgot about the briefcase and 
the gun inside.
The noise of the guns and the 
agonizing screams ceased much  
sooner than expected, and, even 
so, it took Alex a while to get 
his head out from under the table. 
When he did, he found Jack's bar 
turned into a war zone, with 
dozens of severed bodies strewn 
across the floor, walls and 
ceiling. And in front of him, a 
group of four strangers, highly 
cybernetized, and armed to the 
teeth. The one closest to him, 
with a mischievous grin, lit up 
a cigar and asked.
	
—Is this the job interview?
	
—Yes, this is the interview, —
said Alex, a little more relaxed— 
You're just in time.
* V.M.P *
P
layers, when shaping their PCs before starting the 
game, will have eight different classes to choose 
from, representing stereotypes or classes typical of 
the Cyberpunk genre. 
But before players choose their class, they will need 
to establish its basic abilities.
ESTABLISHING ABILITIES
All PCs will have 6 main abilities that will define them. 
These abilities will be the following.
● Strength (STR): The character's ability to lift heavy 
objects, pass strength tests, and cause physical 
damage with both melee weapons and bare 
hands. It is the reference ability for defining those 
actions that require brute strength.
14


● Dexterity (DEX): Defines both agility and accuracy. 
Vital when using ranged weapons (pistols, 
shotguns, submachine guns, rifles or grenades), 
as well as for all those actions requiring athletic 
ability, subtlety and precision. It is the reference 
ability for performing acrobatic, stealthy and 
precise actions.
● Constitution (CON): Measures health and resis- 
tance to continuous physical efforts. It is 
the reference ability for actions that require 
constant effort and for resisting external physical 
aggressions, such as poisons, drugs, polluting 
gases, radiation and diseases.
● Intelligence (INT): Measures the ability to acquire 
new knowledge, languages and to solve problems. 
This ability is also used for hacking or netrunning. 
It is the reference ability for all those skills and 
technical knowledge.
● Wisdom (WIS): It encompasses both intuition and 
the capacity for reflection and creativity based on 
experience. It is the baseline ability for intuiting 
hidden dangers, traps and enemies, performing 
creative actions that do not involve physical action, 
or for tracking characters, targets or clues in 
natural, unexplored or dangerous environments.
● Charisma (CHA): Evaluates the ability to relate to 
other people through personality and character. It 
serves both to convince, seduce or intimidate a 
target. It may also be used to bargain for better 
prices when acquiring equipment, weapons or 
implants. In essence, the reference ability for 
social interactions.
Ability are determined by seven rolls of 3D6. Discard 
the lowest roll and assign the other six to your choice. 
Depending on the value you assign you will get a 
Modifier (MOD) for each ability.

player being able to choose between Bulldog, Cypher, 
Gentleman, Handyman, Recon, Shadow, Output and 
Save.
Each class grants two Talents at Level 1. New Talents are 
acquired as new levels are reached.

CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
•	 Hardware (+INT. or DEX. MOD): All those skills 
focused on the practical use of mechanical devices. 
For example, preparing explosive devices, armory, 
vehicle mechanics, repairing implants, picking 
electronic or deadbolt locks, etc.
•	 Punk (+CHA. or WIS. MOD): This skill gives access 
to the black market and gangster safe zones. It 
includes contacts, reputation and the ease of 
getting anything in the underworld, illegally.
•	 Savage (+WIS. MOD): These are those skills 
that allow survival in non-urban environments, 
and carry out actions such as hunting, tracking, 
climbing, survival, knowledge of flora and fauna, 
and so on. It is also used for tests in biology, 
medicine and other tasks related to living beings 
and organisms. It also allows distinguishing 
between synthetic and natural elements.
•	 Urban (+CHA. or WIS. MOD): Those skills that 
allow to manage in urban environments, providing 
the characters with knowledge on how to survive 
in big cities and their suburbs. It allows to know 
who is who in the asphalt jungle.
•	 Wheels (+DEX. MOD): Possessors of this skill will 
have greater skill and control over land vehicles, 
whether two, four, six or eight wheeled. This skill 
will be added to the Vehicle Maneuverability and 
Speed tests.
 
CHOOSE YOUR SKILLS
All characters start at level 1 with 6 + INT. MOD points 
to allocate among the 10 available skills. Each skill can 
receive a maximum of 2 points at level 1. The maximum 
points that can be reached per skill will be 10 points.
•	 Charm (+CHA. MOD): Whenever there is a social 
interaction involved, such as negotiating the price 
of goods or equipment with a vendor or supplier 
or convincing a character to change his mind or do 
something for us, this skill will be helpful.
•	 Corporate (+CHA. MOD): It encompasses several 
aspects such as, for example, corporate reputation 
and the character's ability to interact with 
members of corporations, access contacts and find 
equipment or information in a legal way.
•	 Cyber (+INT. MOD): Covers all those skills focused 
on computers, programming or handling binary 
data.
•	 Fearless: This skill will be used to intimidate tough 
guys (+CHA. MOD) or to resist stun shock rolls 
(+CON. MOD).
•	 Gunsmith: Characters proficient in this skill will 
increase the value of the attack roll with Medium 
or Long firearms (+DEX. MOD). This skill will also 
be added when a weapon must be unholstered 
(+DEX. MOD).
 
24


CALCULATE THE REMAINING FEATURES
MOVEMENT (MOV)
Indicates the number of meters a character can walk 
during one round (3 seconds) of combat. The movement 
base is 10 + DEX. MOD. in meters.
MOV = 10 + DEX. MOD
In case the character decides to Run, the total MOV 
value will be doubled by two (x2). However, if the 
character runs, he will not be able to perform any 
additional action during his turn. In case of combat, he 
will have additional penalties, (see CHAPTER 3: Combat 
System, page 40).
HIT POINTS (HP)
At level 1, write down the maximum number of the 
Hit Die (HD) of your class (6 for D6, 8 for D8 or 10 for 
D10) and add the CON. MOD.
HP = HD + CON. MOD
DEFENSE (DEF)
Base Defense is 10. The higher the better; the harder 
it will be for your character to take damage during 
combat. Add the CON. MOD and the bonuses for Armor 
and Implants (if any) to get the Defense (DEF) value. 
DEF = Base Defense (10) + CON. MOD
+ Armor + Implants
SOUL
The Soul is made up of four aspects that shape the 
character's personality. When an individual modifies 
his body with an implant, he loses part of his Soul and, 
with it, his creativity, his ability to relate to people, his 
memories and hinders the acquisition of experience 
during the game (see Synthetic Tares, page 26). 
 
Players will have 10 + CHA. MOD + INT. MOD Soul points. 
The Soul points will decrease with each implant grafted 
to the PC. Note: Not all implants will cost the same Soul 
points. 
CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
SOUL = 10 + CHA. MOD + INT. MOD
The loss of Soul does not imply the death or subtraction 
of the character by the GD to turn him into an NPC. 
When a character reaches 0 Soul points he will remain 
human, very similar to a synthetic, but human after all.
 
When a player grafts an implant, in addition to losing 
Soul points, there will be a small chance of acquiring 
Synthetic Tares (page 26).
 
Soul points can be regained if players remove their 
implants in medical clinics (legal or unlicensed), but 
Synthetic Tares cannot be treated or removed.
ATTACK (ATK)
Consult the level advancement table and note the 
ATK Bonus for your Level and Class. For melee attacks 
add, to your ATK Bonus, your STR. MOD, to the value of 
the attack roll. For ranged attacks add your DEX . MOD.
Melee Attack = 1D20
+ ATK Bonus + STR. MOD
Ranged Attack (Firearms, Grenades, etc.) = 
1D20 + ATK Bonus + DEX. MOD
INSTINCTS (INS)
It is a bonus applied to rolls to instinctively avoid 
dangers. For example, if, when entering a building, the 
roof falls down because it is in bad condition, the PC 
must make a roll of 1D20 + CON. MOD (to resist the 
blow) or DEX. MOD (to dodge the blow) + INS BONUS.
 
The roll must overcome a Difficulty Class (DC) imposed 
by the GD. If the player succeeds, he will pass the 
test and come out unscathed. If he fails, he will suffer 
penalties or damage at the GD's discretion.
 
To determine the INS bonus, consult the level 
advancement table and note the bonus for your class.
Instinct Roll = 1D20 + ABILITIES MOD
 + BONUS INS
25


CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
•	 Lack of empathy: Empathy is the cognitive ability of 
a person to understand the emotional universe of 
another and act accordingly. Having this synthetic 
tare implies taking the communication, social 
relations or CHA abilities tests with Disadvantage 
and the loss of social talents.
•	 Inexperience: We define experience as the faculty 
of the human being to have obtained a knowledge 
or skill from an experience or situation. Having 
this synthetic tare doubles the cost of experience 
points needed to level up.
•	 Loss of Memories: Remembering allows a human 
to retain and memorize past events, actions and/
or conversations, as well as access certain data 
and experiences. Having this synthetic tare gives 
Disadvantage to tests of intellectual character, 
knowledge or related to the WIS abilities.
TALENTS
Talents allow to obtain improvements in Skills 
or provide aspects that grant Advantages to the 
characters. Many of them require activation by 
paying PowerUps, while others are passive and do 
not require activation, since they are always working. 
●	 Silent Attack: If you succeed in a stealth challenge 
test against an enemy (page 44), and, when 
moving, you are close enough to reach your 
target, declaring the attack action and spending 
1 PowerUP, you will automatically eliminate your 
target. In case there are two enemies, the second 
enemy may be attacked with Advantage, if the 
player succeeds in passing a second stealth facing 
test.
●  Lucky: At each start of game session you can 
spend 1 PowerUp and make three rolls with 
D20. Record the two highest results on a piece of 
paper. During the game you may change the result 
of a roll to one of those noted on the paper. Such 
a value can only be used once per game session.
●  Aimed Attack: Your attacks become Critical with 
a result of 18-20.
● Biomechanics: You can install an additional 
implant at no Soul point cost.
POWERUPS (POW)
Points that depend on the Soul of each character. If 
you install an implant and your Soul score drops, the 
number of PowerUps available to you will also drop. 
It can never be lower than 0 or higher than your Soul 
score.
POW = SOUL (10 + CHA. MOD 
+ INT. MOD)
They can be spent to perform any test with Advantage, 
nullify a Disadvantage of your own, cause a Disadvantage 
to an opponent or activate those talents that require it.
 
Spent PowerUps are recovered (up to the character's 
current Soul points total) at the start of a new game 
session or after an 8-hour rest.
BACKGROUND
Write in a couple of lines a sentence that defines your 
character's past or a current profession. At levels 6 and 
10 you can add a New Background.
 
Here are some examples: Chief Security Officer of Tech 
Corp, Ex-member of SEAL Team Alpha, Systems Analyst 
at Net Systems, Chief Mechanic of Red Bull Star F-4000, 
Negotiator of Sigma Corp, Kyodai of the Inagawa Clan, 
Member of the Hells Angels, Accountant of the Calabria 
family.
SYNTHETIC TARES
Synthetic Tares can be acquired as a character loses 
Soul points until becoming a quasi-synthetic being and 
will affect abilities tests, Skill tests or Instinct rolls. The 
way to acquire Synthetic Tares will be explained later, in 
CHAPTER 5: Implants (Page 51).
•	 Lack of creativity: Decreases the human faculty 
to create and innovate. Focused on discovering 
new procedures that allow performing activities 
and routines in a different way than usual. Having 
this synthetic tare gives Disadvantage to the tests 
related to the Hardware skill and those of the WIS 
abilities that are based on creating something 
from scratch, as well as in those tests that involve 
the INT abilities.
26


● Good Reputation: A perfect combination of 
overwhelming personality and good luck make 
your actions always look the best. In game 
terms, once per game session you can have 
an Advantage in a communication test and by 
spending 1 PowerUp you can avoid an armed or 
social conflict outright, because they recognize 
your reputation and give you that chance.
● Courage: You have considerable willpower that 
makes you stoically face danger and certain 
situations that could damage your mental health. 
You get a +2 to the Mental Health roll (page 46) 
to see if you are affected by the scene you are 
experiencing.
● Elegant: Style is your trademark. Your good suits 
and distinction help you in social interactions. 
All communication tests at meetings, social 
events, and jet-setting parties are performed with 
Advantage by virtue of the character's exquisite 
physical appearance.
●  Mechanical Empathy: You are good with machines, 
even when you barely know their functions. 
When you manipulate a machine of which you are 
ignorant of its workings, the Disadvantage does 
not influence you and you can take the test without 
positive Abilities or Skill modifiers. If you spend 1 
PowerUp, you gain proficiency in its operation and 
can apply the corresponding Skill in future uses. 
● Swordsman: Whenever a player uses edged 
weapons in combat (this includes knives, razors, 
swords, axes or shurikens), in the event of rolling 
a critical on an attack roll, spending 2 PowerUp, 
the Critical Damage of the weapon's Damage Roll 
will be multiplied by 2 (x2).
● Ghost: Whenever a PC wanders or wanders 
through dimly lit scenarios or locations, he will 
have a +4 bonus to Stealth opposed rolls; if the 
location, instead, is well-lit, he will get the same 
bonus by spending 1 PowerUp.
● Medical Training: When you use a medical kit you 
directly heal as many HP as possible from the 
dice roll. You can spend 1 PowerUp to stabilize 
someone with 0 HPs for 10 extra rounds.
CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
● Entrepreneurial Momentum: You live life with 
intensity. You gain an additional Background.
● Fight with X: Substitute X for a melee or fire 
weapon. When used it gives a +1 to the attack roll 
and damage roll. It also negates the Disadvantage 
when using two weapons of the same type 
simultaneously, provided the rules for two-
weapon combat allow it (see CHAPTER 3: Combat 
System, page 38).
●  Military Leader: If a PC decides to hire a mercenary 
and his loyalty is shaken, when making the Loyalty 
roll, he will add a +2 point bonus to his roll. For 
each level above 1, the PC will add 1 more point 
to the roll.
● Steel Muscles: The character has an enviable 
musculature that helps reduce the effects of 
recoil caused by firearms. By acquiring this 
talent the character cancels the Automatic Fire 
Disadvantage and turns it into a -1 modifier. In 
addition, it increases damage in melee combat 
by +1.
27


CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
● Sixth Sense: Your character has the ability to 
perceive those situations that may be negative 
or involve some danger to your health and 
integrity. By spending 1 PowerUp, the GD will 
confirm your suspicions, though without giving 
any details as to what kind of dangers you may 
encounter. Any class with a Soul score of 0 may 
not acquire or use this talent.
● Sharpshooter: Long Weapon Attack rolls will 
add a +3 bonus to the attack roll (by spending 
1 PowerUP) or may be made with Advantage 
(Player's choice).
TABLE OF PROGRESS BY LEVEL 
With each successful combat, objective or mission, 
the GD will award Experience Points (EP) that will vary 
according to the challenges overcome by the PCs. When 
the PCs get a certain number of EP, they will level up.
 
This will lead to an increase in their Attack (ATK) and 
Instinct (INS) bonuses, depending on the level they 
are in, as well as being able to improve the value of 
the Abilities, by being able to add a certain number of 
points when reaching certain levels according to each 
class. The Abilities points can be freely added to any 
of the PCs abilities, without restrictions, either a single 
point to a Abilities or spread among several of them. 
This increase will be linked to a possible increase in the 
MOD associated to the Abilities, so the PC will have to 
adjust it.
 
If the Synthetic Tare Inexperience is present, the EP 
needed to reach a new level will be doubled.
 
For each level increase, the PC will roll the Hit Die (HD), 
indicated in the character class, and the result will be 
added to the Total HP before the increase. This result 
will be the Total HP after leveling up.
 
For each level up, 2 points will also be awarded to the 
skills, either to add it to one already possessed by the 
PC or to acquire a new one, taking into account that the 
value of no skill can exceed 10 points.
Characters can acquire a new Talent, choosing any from 
the general list when they reach levels 4 and 8. When 
the character reaches levels 6 and 10 they can select a 
new Background.
● Handyman: Machines hold no secrets for you. 
You are an expert at both adding implants and 
making repairs. You can repair anything. You gain 
a +2 to Hardware tests. On implant repairs, you 
can spend 1 PowerUp and directly repair the 
damage.
●  Iron Mind: You have a trained mind to resist any 
intrusion. Attempts to interrogate, intimidate, 
convince or confuse, by an enemy, are always 
performed with Disadvantage. In case you are 
carrying a Cypher, by spending 1 PowerUp you 
can ignore any first attack made by a Black Ice.
●  Machine Mind: You can control two drones from 
a single BCI. If you give them the same command, 
they can both act by spending a single action. If 
you want them to do different things, you will 
have to decide each turn which one you control. 
Also, if one of your drones is going to be reduced 
to 0 HPs, you can spend 1 PowerUp to ignore the 
damage from that attack, although the drone is 
disabled until you can spend a few minutes to 
reset it.
● Golden Peak: You are very skilled at talking, able 
to confuse or convince anyone. When you try 
to convince someone, you take the CHA. test 
with Advantage. In the corporate realm, you can 
spend 1 PowerUp to gain a direct success in a 
negotiation. Any character who has the synthetic 
trait "Lack of empathy" cannot acquire or use 
this talent.
●  Plugin: You have a knack for connecting to Node 
Constellations. On the first connection to a node 
you perform the Cyber test with Advantage.
●  Contact Network: You have a very wide network 
of contacts all over the world. If you pass a 
roll with a DC=15 in the skills Urban, Punk or 
Corporate (depending on the environment) you 
will find a contact. With 1 PowerUp you will 
automatically succeed.
●  Keen Sense: Whenever an Instincts or Wisdom 
test is required in which the selected sense is 
the primary sense (sight, hearing, smell, taste or 
touch), you can make the roll with Advantage.
28


At this point they are considered to be people of great 
relevance in the world around them and have as much 
fame as enemies who hate them. Apart from the normal 
benefits granted by any background, these can provide 
additional benefits and commitments in the form of 
favors and obligations to corporations, archenemies, 
business debts, and so on. All under the supervision of 
the GD.

ACCORDING TO TESTS PASSED

depending on the test he has passed. There will be 
dozens, hundreds of situations not contemplated in this 
table, so you can use it as a reference to award EP.

CHAPTER 1: CHARACTER CREATION
As Background, Carla decides to give her character a 
military past: "Clive is a war veteran, a born survivor, 
who has lived through and seen more conflicts, civil wars 
and massacres than he would like to remember". 
 
For initial equipment, Carla decides to opt for a 
Commando Assault Rifle pack, 3 magazines, Tactical 
Vest, paramilitary clothing and 300 credits (as a result 
of rolling 1D4 and multiplying the result by 100). Watch 
out! As Clive is equipped with a tactical vest, he will add 
a +2 armor bonus to his defense, being the DEF 12 (10 
+ 0 + 2).
EXAMPLE OF LEVELING UP
Carla has completed her first game session with 
satisfaction. The GD has set up an interesting adventure, 
balanced, with different challenges and some combats. 
The game went so well that Carla got enough EPs to 
level up.
 
At Level 2, for the Bulldog class, the ATK bonus will be 
worth 1 point, and the INS bonus will be worth 2 points.
 
He will also get 1 point of Abilities, which he will award 
to INT, increasing its value to 8. With this update of the 
INT Abilities, he will modify his INT. MOD, now worth 
-1. This will be associated with an alteration in other 
associated statistics, such as Soul, which, depending 
on the INT and CHA. MODs, will now have a value of 
7 points (10 + CHA. MOD + INT. MOD = 10 - 1 -2). The 
same will happen with POW, which will now have 7 
points.
 
The level increase leads to an increase in HP, which will 
be updated by rolling the HD of the Bulldog class. Carla 
will roll 1D10, and will get a 6, which, adding this result 
to the total HP she had at level 1, will get a 16 (10+6).
 
With these simple steps Carla will have upgraded her PC 
to Level 2.
EXAMPLE OF CHARACTER CREATION 
Carla is going to start a game session of Nights of 
Chrome, but before that, she must give shape to her 
character. Carla is a determined girl who likes to face 
problems without contemplation, so she decides to 
create an action character. Carla opts for the Bulldog 
class for her game, which she will call Clive.
 
Carla needs to know the value of Clive's main abilities, so 
she makes 7 rolls of 3D6 and gets the following results: 
11, 4, 14, 12, 7, 11, 6. She decides to discard the lowest 
value, 4, and keeps the rest. With the results distributed 
among the abilities, he also obtains the MODs:
STR: 	
	
12 	
	
(+1)
DEX: 	
	
14 	
	
(+2)
CON: 	
	
11 	
	
(+0)
INT: 	
	
07 	
	
(-2)
WIS: 	
	
11 	
	
(+0)
CHA: 	
	
06 	
	
(-2)
Carla, will have 4 skill points (6 + INT. MOD = 6 -2), so 
she decides to give 2 points to the Fearless skill and 2 
points to the Gunsmith skill.
 
Carla, by choosing the Bulldog class, gets the initial 
talents of Fight with X or Steel Muscles (having to 
choose one of them) and Sixth Sense. She decides to opt 
for the talents Fight with Long Weapon and Sixth Sense.
 
As for the rest of the values, Carla would obtain the 
following results:
•	 MOV (10 + DEX. MOD = 10 + 2) = 12 meters per 
round.
•	 HP (HD + CON. MOD = 10 + 0) = 10 HP
•	 DEF (10 + CON. MOD + Armor + Implants = 10 + 0) 
= 10 DEF
•	 SOUL (10 + CHA. MOD + INT. MOD = 10 -2 -2) = 6 
Soul Points
•	 ATK (At Level 1) = 0 points Bon ATK
•	 INS (At Level 1) = +1 points INS
•	 POW (SOUL) = 6 points POW

CHAPTER 2: GAME SYSTEM
CHAPTER 2: GAME SYSTEM
T
he 
projection, 
which 
illuminated a smoky, darkened 
room, displayed graphs, maps, 
and reports full of data in an 
almost unintelligible language. A 
man in a suit, known as Smith, paced 
in front of the projection and 
explained the display to a group 
of mercenaries while pointing a 
laser pointer at the highlights 
that emerged from the projection. 
But no one in the darkened room 
paid any attention to the suit's 
endless chatter.
None of what was being explained 
had the slightest relevance.
 
No one paid attention until the 
man with the shaved skull and 
tattooed skin intervened.
	
—All this stuff you're telling 
us is all very well, but... it's 
all bullshit, you know?
	
—Fuck yes, yes! —said a man 
in a military uniform with an 
ocular implant, sitting in the 
background.
	
—What does he say is bullshit? 
—asked 
the 
Smith—. 
Tell 
us, 
sir...?
	
—Raul.
	
—Raul, yes, tell us what should 
be important and noteworthy to 
those assembled here?
	
—What should be important? In 
my opinion?
	
—Yes —said Smith as he walked 
over to a desk and sat on the 
edge—. In his opinion.
	
—Very easy, man. First: what 
you're going to pay us; second: 
show us the equipment you're going 
to leave us for the assignment; 
third: where the target is; fourth: 
whether there will be special  
 
33


CHAPTER 2: GAME SYSTEM
bonuses if we're efficient with 
the job.
 
	
—Well, efficiency is a given, 
ha! —Smith laughed—. I don't think 
there's any need for a Special 
bonus.
	
—Efficiency 
is 
not 
only 
carrying out the job successfully, 
but also not leaving more stiffs 
than necessary, nor turning the 
area into a fucking smoking crater 
—said Raul smugly.
Laughter 
erupted 
among 
those 
present.
 
	
—You're too much, Mr. Raul. 
It's guys like you who are the 
first to get screwed when they 
have to carry out contracts of a 
certain complexity.
 
The laughter died down and some 
nervous coughing could be heard 
in the back of the room.
 
	
—Yes, I admit it —Smith said 
as he walked in front of the 
projection with his arms crossed—. 
You're up to your eyeballs in 
cyber-implants, drugs and hacking 
programs. You've done well in the 
business, you've been successful, 
yes. You've done some big jobs 
and made a name for yourselves in 
the underworld. You've had a few 
scares, yes, but that has made 
you wiser and more experienced. 
However, none of that will do for 
this job. Now you're playing with 
the grown-ups, and your cockiness 
and bullshit won't do you any 
good. Is that clear to you, kids?
 
Raul said nothing, and the rest 
remained silent. Smith looked at 
the projection, and changed slides 
with a blink of his eyes.
 
	
—The target is Ryuku Soho, the 
regional sales manager of Prix 
Industries. You must access his 
offices, on the 50th floor of the 
Gommo building, and extract him 
safely... or at least his head —
the mercenaries laughed with the 
comment—. How to proceed? In the 
documents we have given you there 
are reports on the building and 
possible escape routes, but, in 
short, I suggest the roof, taking 
the helicopter waiting there, or 
the sewers... as long as they 
don't mind getting a little dirty.
 
	
—What about security personnel? 
—asked a woman with a mechanical 
arm for an implant, sitting in 
the front row.
 
	
—Experienced, well-armed and 
numerous. These guys are not 
like the two-bit thugs you deal 
with on a regular basis. They're 
tough, trigger-happy guys who 
won't hesitate to empty a whole 
load into you as soon as they get 
a shot at you. Well, you have the 
reports for you to study, but if 
you have any doubts, now is the 
time to ask me.
 
The mercenaries looked at each 
other, uncomfortable and silent. 
An old soldier, with a scraggly 
beard and a worn kevlar vest, 
raised his hand.
 
	
—Yes? —Smith asked.
 
	
—I have a dry throat, you know? 
Where I could have a beer before 
you send us to the slaughterhouse?
 
* V.M.P *
34


T
he most important rule is to apply common sense. 
Whenever a situation, event, activity or test of 
relevance to the adventure occurs, and which may 
involve a possibility of failure for the PC, with possible 
mishaps or damage, the GD will request a roll to pass 
that test. The GD will assign a Difficulty Class to the test, 
(from now on abbreviated as DC). The PC must roll 1D20 
and add a modifier (MOD) for the abilities associated 
with the test he is taking, and if necessary the score of a 
related Skill, at the GDs discretion. If the result obtained 
is greater than or equal to the DC set by the GD, the 
test will be considered passed successfully. If the result 
is less than the DC, the test is considered failed and the 
GD describes the result of the failure.
A natural score of 20 will always be a success and a score 
of 1 will always be a failure.
COMMON DIFFICULTIES
●	 Simple or very easy task: Is it really necessary to 
make a roll?
● Complex or rushed task: DC > 11
● Difficult task or under stress: DC > 14
● Very difficult task or under a lot of stress: DC > 17
● Extremely difficult or time-sensitive task: DC > 20
BACKGROUNDS
A player may claim that one of his backgrounds gives 
him Advantage in the performance of a test or event. 
If there is some relationship between the test and the 
background, the GD may award an additional +2 bonus. 
If there is a lot of relationship, he may allow the roll to 
be made with Advantage.
ADVANTAGE AND DISADVANTAGE 
During the game there may be situations in which a 
talent, an implant or a scene described by the GD allows 
the dice to be rolled with Advantage or Disadvantage. 
When this happens, roll 2D20. When acting with 
Advantage, the higher result will be chosen and with 
Disadvantage the lower. If an action has simultaneously 
Advantage and Disadvantage, both cancel each other 
out, thus rolling a single D20.
CHAPTER 2: GAME SYSTEM
FALL DAMAGE 
Characters will suffer 1D6 points of damage for each 
fall higher than 4 meters. Each additional 4 meters will 
add an additional 1D6 to the roll. A fall higher than 20 
meters will be an instant death.
 
TIME CONTROL
The GD will determine how time is spent within the 
game world. However, combat is usually divided into 
rounds (3 seconds), while action time will be divided 
into turns (10 minutes). In this way, it is easy to control, 
for example, when a lithium battery for the flashlight 
runs out (1 hour) or the fuel for a hovercraft (4 hours).
REST
The PCs will be able to rest to recover HP. Every 
4 hours of rest, the PC will recover half of the HP lost 
during an assignment, job or combat. If he rests for 8 
hours, he will recover the total HP.
 
To rest, he will have to rest in a bed or rest capsule. The 
price to rent a room in a standard hotel usually costs 
between 250-300 credits.
 
If the PCs don't have enough money to spend (or do not 
feel like doing it) they can try to rest in other improvised 
and less comfortable beds such as a sleeping bag, a 
cardboard box or a garbage can. But be careful! If a PC 
decides to sleep in this kind of "beds", he will be able to 
recover the lost HP, yes, but he will only gain half EPs 
for the rest of the game session.
 
Any place where a PC can rest and that is not a hostel or 
hotel, will have this penalty in terms of gaining EPs.
 
ALTERNATIVE RULE: If you want to make the awakening 
of the PCs something more interesting, when sleeping in 
places that are not traditional hostels or hotels, the PCs, 
when waking up, must make a 1D20 roll. If the result is 
less than 5, they will discover that a group of thugs will 
have decided to assault them while they were sleeping, 
starting a combat. The GD will have complete freedom 
to choose the type of enemy and the number that the 
PCs will have to face.
35


CHAPTER 2: GAME SYSTEM
1D20 + Wheels Skill
+ Maneuverability Bonus + VBR
vs
DC GD
If the player succeeds, he will be able to make the 
speed roll. In case of failing the roll, there will be 
two possible situations.
o  Failure of the roll: It means a driving error 
and automatic loss of the subsequent speed 
roll.
o	 Critical Failure: The character will crash the 
vehicle completely failing in the pursuit.
•	 The speed roll consists of rolling 1D20, adding the 
Wheels skill value, the Speed bonus of the vehicle 
being driven, subtracting Vehicle Body Resistance 
(VBR), and adding any other applicable modifiers. 
The result of the player's roll must be higher than 
the opposing vehicle's face roll to succeed. A 
successful result on the part of the pursuer will 
result in a one position decrease in distance to his 
opponent. In case of failure, he will increase the 
distance to his opponent by one position.
1D20 + Wheels Skill 
+ Speed bonus  - VBR [Player]
vs
1D20 + Wheels Skill 
+ Speed bonus  - VBR [NPC Opponent]
In addition, drivers may decide to change the way they 
drive:
•	 Put the pedal to the metal: The driver decides to 
forget caution and step on the accelerator without 
fear. He gains an additional +2 to his Speed bonus, 
but loses the Maneuverability bonus.
•	 Dawdle: The driver decides not to take any risk 
and slows down to avoid crashing. He gains an 
additional +2 to his Maneuverability bonus but 
loses the Speed bonus.
VEHICLES
CHASES AND ESCAPES 
The roads and alleys of the city often become the 
scenes of dangerous and fast-paced chases. Each vehicle 
has two indicators that act as bonuses to the Hardware 
skill tests (see vehicle list on page 88).
•	 Maneuverability: How agile and maneuverable 
the vehicle is.
•	 Speed: How fast the vehicle is. It is not its actual 
speed, but its ability to run.
 
The GD must determine the distance at which the 
vehicles involved start. There are four distances, with 
the last distance indicating the end of the chase or race. 
The distances are:
•	 Impact Distance: In this distance the vehicles 
collide with each other and are equal in speed 
and position. They can ram each other by making 
driving rolls against each other. Whoever wins the 
confrontation will drive his opponent off the road 
and the pursuit will be concluded.
•	 Short Distance: TThis is a distance at which the 
pursuer will be close to catching up with his target, 
but not close enough to run him off the road.
•	 Medium Distance: The difficulty in following the 
vehicle and shooting at it will be very high and the 
possibility of losing it during the pursuit will be 
great.
•	 Distance out of Range: Reaching it by the fleeing 
driver will mean winning the race and escaping.
The two drivers involved must make a driving roll and a 
speed roll in each round:
•	 The driving roll consists of rolling 1D20, adding 
the Wheels skill value, the Maneuverability 
bonus of the vehicle being driven, the Vehicle 
Body Resistance (VBR), and any other applicable 
modifiers, against a DC marked by the GD (based 
on traffic congestion, route complexity, or any 
other aspect that influences maneuverability). 
36


BODY RESISTANCE
During a chase, vehicles may collide by means of 
opposed driving rolls as long as they are within Impact 
Distance. However, not all vehicles will ram or resist 
collisions with the same force. Depending on the 
vehicle, they will have different Vehicle Body Resistance 
(VBR) points, with this value being added to the driving 
rolls against each other. The higher the VBR value, the 
higher the probability of winning the encounter.
 
The VBR value will be associated to the Vehicle Armor 
Value (VAV). For each point of VBR the VAV will be 10. 
So a vehicle that has an VBR of 4, will have 40 points 
of VAV. The VAV points will not be taken into account 
during chases but during combats and shootouts, as 
vehicles can be used as cover.
 
For every 10 points of VAV lost in a firefight, you will 
lose 1 point of VBR. VAV points may also be lost during 
a chase if he loses a confrontational driving roll. Once 
off the road, and having lost the chase, the GD must roll 
1D10 to determine how many VBR points the vehicle 
will have lost. If this value reaches 0 the vehicle will be 
disabled. Note: Just because a vehicle goes off the road 
does not mean that it is automatically disabled. As long 
as there are VBR points left, the vehicle can be used to 
move, but the pursuit will be considered failed.
 
During chases, the PCs can make attack rolls at a 
distance, in the same way as if they were in a combat 
on foot. What DC must they pass to determine if they 
succeed in the attack roll? A DC associated with the 
distance from the target.
 
Whenever the attacker succeeds in the attack roll, he 
will be able to make a damage roll. This result will be 
subtracted from VAV.
 
NOTE: Remember that for every 10 points of VAV lost, 
the vehicle will lose 1 point of VBR.
 
VAV points lost during a street shooting will also be 
reflected in the VBR, in case the vehicle is used for a 
subsequent pursuit.
 
As long as the PCs go to a mechanic or garage, before 
the car becomes unusable, and pay for the repair, they 
will recover all VBR points.
CHAPTER 2: GAME SYSTEM
OPTIONAL RULE: BAGGAGE AND WEIGHT
In principle, the PCs have no load limitations on the 
number of weapons, equipment or objects they can 
carry in their inventory. But, if the GD and the players 
want to give more weight to the inventory management, 
they can apply this small rule.
 
All PCs can only carry three weapons: one melee 
weapon, and two firearms, which can be two short 
weapons, two medium weapons, one short weapon and 
one long weapon or one short weapon and one heavy 
weapon.
 
As for ammunition and grenades, you may carry a 
maximum of 3 magazines of each weapon, and 3 
assorted grenades.
 
As for armor equipment, you can carry the one you 
have on you, and a spare in your inventory.
 
And of the rest of the equipment (medical, drugs and 
assorted equipment), he can carry a maximum of 15 
plus the value of the CON characteristic in objects. If, for 
example, a PC has a CON of 8, he can carry a maximum 
of 23 items (15+8) in his inventory.
INVENTORY = 15 + CON. ABILITY
NOTE: If an PC has several objects of the same type, they 
can be grouped as one, saving space in the inventory. 
So if an PC has four electronic picks, he will not occupy 
4 different objects (4 different electronic picks), but 4 
electronic picks grouped in 1 inventory object.

CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
o	 If the PC is the target of an attack roll by an 
enemy NPC, on the turn following the Run 
action, he will add 2 points to his DEF.
o	 A PC or NPC who performs a Run action will 
have a subsequent penalty. On his next turn, 
before making any type of roll (be it Abilities, 
Skill, Attack, etc.), he must make a 1D20 roll 
plus his CON. MOD (DC=15) to find out if he 
regains his breath, after having made the run 
action on his previous turn. If he succeeds, 
he may make any roll without penalty. In 
case of failure, during said turn, he will make 
the rolls with Disadvantage.
•	 1 Movement Action + Reload Weapon.
•	 1 Movement Action + 1 Heal Action.
•	 1 Movement Action + 1 Abilities/Skill Roll.
•	 1 Attack roll + Reload Weapon.
•	 1 Attack roll + 1 Heal Action.
•	 1 Attack roll + 1 Abilities/Skill roll.
•	 1 Movement Action + Reload Weapon + 1 attack 
roll (but with Disadvantage).
The GD may authorize more than two actions to be 
performed during a combat turn, provided that one of 
them is performed with Disadvantage.
ATTACK ROLL 
To determine whether an attack hits a target, an 
attack roll must be made, which will vary depending 
on whether it is a ranged or firearm attack or a melee 
attack.
MELEE ATTACK
In melee combat, the attacker, when choosing a 
target, must roll 1D20, add his ATK bonus corresponding 
to the character's level, and add the STR. MOD. If the 
result is greater than or equal to the target's DEF value, 
he will have succeeded. If he is striking with his bare 
fists, the damage roll he must make is 1D4. In case he is 
using a melee weapon, he must make a damage roll for 
the value indicated by the weapon used.
T
he whole plan devised by the PJs has gone down 
the drain. An undetected trap, a stronger lock than 
usual, a misunderstanding during a conversation... 
whatever. The events have been precipitated and it's 
time to fight. Whether with firearms or melee weapons, 
it's time for the PCs to measure their strength against 
the ruthless enemies armed to the teeth. The time for 
the cuddles has come!
PHASES OF COMBAT
FOR EACH PARTICIPANT
•	 Initiative Roll (only once, at the beginning of the 
combat, for each participant).
•	 Perform action, movement or attack roll.
o Attack roll (melee or ranged).
o Damage roll (in case of success in the attack 
roll).
•	 The target of the Attack Roll will deduct HP in case 
of having suffered damage from the attack roll.
•	 Restart all phases of the combat the next 
participant according to the initiative order.
•	 When all the participants have acted, another 
round of combat will start (no need to repeat the 
initiative roll). The combat will end when one of 
the two groups has lost all its combatants.
INITIATIVE ROLL 
In combat, action is by order of Initiative. To determine 
this order, each player will roll 1D20 and add the DEX. 
MOD and any other bonus that improves the initiative. 
The PCs and NPCs will act from highest to lowest score, 
and this order will be maintained until the end of the 
combat. NOTE: In case of a tie between PC and NPC, the 
PC will act first.
COMBAT ACTIONS 
Players, during combat, may perform different actions 
during their turn:
•	 1 Movement Action + 1 attack roll.
•	 ½ Movement Action + 1 attack roll + ½ Movement 
Action.
•	 Run (Movement x 2): You may only perform this 
action during your turn. Keep in mind that:

vs

+ Armor + Enemy NPCs Implants
The result of the damage roll will be subtracted from the 
enemy NPCs HP.
FIREARMS COMBAT
In combat with firearms or ranged weapons, the 
attacker, when choosing a target, must roll 1D20, add 
his ATK bonus corresponding to the character's level, 
and add the DEX. MOD. If the enemy target is behind 
partial cover (+4) he will add that value to his DEF. If the 
target is running he will add 2 points to his DEF. If the 
result is greater than or equal to the DEF value of the 
target, he will have succeeded. The attacker must make 
a damage roll for the value indicated by the weapon 
used. The result of the damage roll will be subtracted 
from the target's HP.
1D20 + ATK Bonus + DEX. MOD
vs
DEF = Base Defense (10) + CON. MOD
+ Armor + Implants + (Partial Cover Value or
2 points for Running) of the Enemy NPCs
RANGE OF FIREARMS OR REMOTE WEAPONS
Firearms or ranged weapons have effective distances 
that improve their chances of impact as the attacker 
approaches his target. The distance in meters varies 
according to the type of weapon, which are specified in 
CHAPTER 8: Weapons, Armor and Equipment, page 80, 
except for the distance "Point Blank" which will always 
be 2 meters or less:
•	 Point Blank (2 meters or less): At this distance all 
weapons get Advantage on the attack roll.
•	 Optimal: This is the ideal range for the weapon: 
the distance between the weapon and the target 
is between the optimal range of the weapon and 
the point blank distance.
•	 Extreme: Shots beyond the optimal range of the 
weapon are fired with Disadvantage.
•	 Out of Range: It is not possible to shoot beyond 
twice the optimal range of the weapon.
CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
FIGHTING WITH TWO WEAPONS
The PC can use two weapons at the same time adding 
the relevant modifiers such as STR, DEX, talents and 
implants.
 
The player will make an attack test for each weapon 
held, rolling 1D20, adding the ATK bonus and the STR 
(if melee) or DEX (if ranged) modifiers, but making each 
attack roll with Disadvantage, as long as the Fighting 
talent with X is not available. If the attack equals or 
exceeds the target's DEF the damage for each weapon 
is determined independently.
 
You can only fight with two melee weapons if they 
are both small weapons or medium weapons (or a 
combination of them). You can only shoot with two 
firearms if they are both small weapons.

CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
will not be able to use the weapon on your action turn. 
If you wish, you can try again to free the casing in the 
following turns, but if in any of the attempts you get a 
Critical Failure (a 1), the weapon will become unusable. 
A weapon that has been rendered unserviceable must 
be taken to a gunsmith or weapons dealer to have the 
casing fixed for a specific price depending on the type of 
weapon.
Each weapon will have a certain Jammed Value (JV) that 
will raise the difficulty to fix the lock-up during combat 
(DC=15 + JV). For example, if a weapon has a +1 JV, in 
the event of a jamming, the player must pass a roll of 
DC=16 with DEX, instead of DC=15 to fix the jamming.
DISGUISE WEAPONS
In certain situations, players will be required to enter 
places with a strict weapons check. Players may attempt 
to pass weapons the moment they are frisked, by making 
an opposed DEX roll against the Tracking Bonus of the 
NPCs who is frisking them. If it is the case that they are 
crossing a security arch, the DEX roll will be against a DC 
determined by the security level.
However, the PCs must keep in mind that, depending on 
the weapon they are carrying, and which they are trying 
to hide from the authorities, they will have a Negative 
Disguise Value (NDV). This value will vary depending 
on how bulky, exotic or conspicuous the weapon is. 
When taking the test, the NDV will be subtracted from 
the player's total roll, and may impair the test, causing 
him to fail, and the weapon he is trying to hide to be 
detected.
1D20 + PCs DEX. MOD - NDV
vs
1D20 + NPCs Tracking Bonus or DC
FIRE SELECTOR
There are several models of firearms, and depending 
on the weapon, they may have three different Firing 
Modes. The Three Round Burst (TRB) and Automatic 
Fire (AF) firing modes will have a specific penalty that 
will be applied at the time of the attack roll, as well as 
the ammunition cost associated with each of the firing 
modes.
•	 Single shot (SS): It is performed as a normal attack. 
This shooting mode has no recoil (penalty) and 
consumes one bullet from the weapon's magazine.
•	 Three Round Burst (TRB): It is performed as a 
normal attack, but 3 bullets are fired at a time 
and a -2 penalty is applied to the attack roll. A 
minimum of 3 bullets in the magazine is required 
to perform a burst. Damage is calculated using the 
roll indicated in the weapon description.
•	 Automatic Fire (AF): Allows you to fire a series 
of 5 bullets by performing an attack roll with 
Disadvantage. A minimum of 5 bullets in the 
magazine is required to fire a AF. With this firing 
mode, the damage is calculated using the roll 
indicated in the description of the weapon and 
must be distributed equally among the targets. 
A maximum of 5 targets may be chosen that are 
close to each other and have a DEX less than or 
equal to the value of the attack roll obtained.
JAMMED
When making an attack roll with firearms, in case 
of getting a 1 result, the weapon will be locked. The PC 
must roll 1D20, add his DEX. MOD, plus the Gunsmith 
skill (in case he has it), to pass a DC=15 to unholster the 
weapon during combat. A failed roll indicates that you

ON WEAPONS RESTRICTIONS
Depending on which class the PCs choose, they may 
have restrictions on using certain weapons. This 
does not mean that a class cannot use a weapon 
for which it is not trained, but that, when making 
the Attack Roll, it will do so with Disadvantage. 
In case of success in the roll, when making the 
Damage Roll, the result will be divided by two (1 
being the minimum value of the Damage Roll). For 
example, if a PC embodying a Cypher, decides to 
use long firearms, the Attack Roll will be made with 
Disadvantage, since the Cypher class is not used to 
this type of weaponry.
42


PROTECTION WITH 
MELEE WEAPONS
During a combat, a player wielding a melee weapon 
may decide to make a defensive move to parry a strike 
from his opponent. If he decides to use his weapon to 
counter the enemy attack, he must declare the action 
and add the Weapon Defense Value (WDV) to his DEF 
value (10 + CON. MOD + Armor + Implants). 
 
If the defending player succeeds, the enemy will miss the 
attack, and will be destabilized on his next turn, so he will 
make his next roll (of any type) with Disadvantage. If the 
defending PC fails, in addition to suffering the damage 
inflicted by the enemy, the weapon he is wielding, and 
which he has used to defend himself, will lose 1 point 
of Weapon Stability Value (WSV). If the WSV reaches 
0, the weapon will break and cannot be repaired. The 
repair value of the weapon will be indicated in the 
weaponry section.
COVERAGE
They are all those objects (vehicles, the door of a 
refrigerator, urban furniture or others) and structures 
that offer shelter to the characters that seek to protect 
themselves from the bullets of their enemies. There is a 
premise that says "if they can't see you, they can't shoot 
you".
There are three types of coverage:
•	 No cover: No bonus to DEF.
•	 Partial: Covers a large part of the PCs body and 
grants a +4 bonus to DEF as long as he stays behind 
the protection.
CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
•	 Total: The target remains hidden from the attacker 
and the attacker cannot shoot him. Additionally, 
the character that during a combat performs a run 
action (MOV x2) improves his DEF by +2.  
OPTIONAL COVERAGE RULE NO. 1:
Moving from Partial Cover to Full Cover.
Players who are sheltered behind Partial Cover, may try 
to hide more intensely (by squeezing the body behind 
the cover or ducking) to achieve Full Cover, declaring 
such action in advance. However, this will have a 
penalty: when the players are going to make an attack 
roll, by leaning out and getting out of cover, they will 
make the roll with Disadvantage, in addition to suffering 
a -2 penalty to the roll itself.
OPTIONAL COVERAGE RULE NO. 2:
Cover Value (CV)
The players, during combat, must take into account that 
the cover has a certain value of resistance to impacts 
and attacks (both melee weapons and firearms), called 
Cover Value (hereinafter CV). Depending on the material 
the cover is made of, the CV will vary.
 
When a PC or NPC attacks a target hidden behind cover, if 
the roll fails, he must still make a damage roll. The value 
of the damage roll will be subtracted from the CV. When 
the CV drops to 0, the cover will be disabled and the 
target will be exposed and will have to look for another 
cover to dodge the attacks. Disabled cover cannot be 
repaired. If the target is hiding behind a vehicle, the VAV 
statistic will be used to know how many CV points he 
has.

CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
STEALTH MOVEMENTS 
A PC, when crossing a place with guards or surveillance, 
may want to do so without attracting attention, avoiding 
to engage in combat. To do so, he must make a stealth 
roll.
The PC must make a stealth roll against the NPC guarding 
an area or on guard. To do so, he will roll 1D20, add his 
DEX. MOD and his Ghost talent bonus (if he has it), 
against 1D20, plus the enemy NPCs Tracking Bonus. If 
the PC succeeds on the roll, he will go unnoticed, and will 
travel as far as his MOV value without being discovered. 
If not, the PC will fail to go undetected, and a combat 
may be initiated.  
Stealth Roll = 1D20 + DEX. MOD
+ Ghost talent bonus (if I had it)
vs
1D20 + Enemy NPC Tracking Bonus
What if the PC prefers to run (MOVx2), when making 
the stealth roll? In that case, the PC roll will be made 
with Disadvantage and the NPC with Advantage.
What would happen, if in the area, there were two, 
three or more NPCs watching, and they were relatively 
close? In this case, the stealth roll would not need to 
be made one by one, it would only be made against the 
NPC with the highest Tracking Bonus of the three. If the 
PC passes the stealth roll, he will pass the stealth roll for 
all three NPCs.
GRENADE LAUNCHING 
During combat, PCs can throw grenades at a target, 
with the attack roll working similarly to that of firearms. 
In case of a failed attack roll, they will make a 1D10 roll 
to determine where the grenade lands and a 1D20 roll 
to determine the distance in meters.
WHY ARE POINTS ONLY SUBTRACTED FROM 
THE CV WHEN THE ATTACK ROLL IS FAILED?
In the context of combat, a successful attack roll is 
assumed to be an accurate roll, a roll that hits the bull's-
eye, not one that passes through or hits the target 
through cover. By this assumption, on a successful attack 
roll, no points are subtracted from the CV of the cover. 
Only on failure, it is assumed in the context of combat, 
that the attack roll has hit the cover squarely, not the 
target of the roll.
 
This logic also serves to compensate for attack roll 
failures: the one making the attack roll will fail, yes, but 
the target will progressively lose the cover he is using.
CRITICALS
An attack roll is considered critical if the result is a 
natural 20. Whenever a Critical is rolled, the roll is 
considered successful. The most usual is to choose to 
cause the maximum damage of the weapon +1, in the 
damage roll.
 
Another option is for the attacker to propose a special 
consequence derived from his attack. Players may 
describe any consequence they wish, although to keep 
the game within reasonable margins, the idea is that 
mechanically it should be represented as one of the 
following options:
•	 The loss of a piece of equipment such as, for 
example, a weapon or Medpack.
•	 That the protection is damaged, reducing the DEX 
by one point for the rest of the combat.
•	 The next Attack Roll received by the target target 
is made with Advantage.
CRITICAL FAILURE
An attack is considered a failure if the result is a 
natural 1. If it was when making a ranged attack with 
firearms, the weapon will suffer a temporary jamming 
(see explanation on page 42). In the case of melee 
combat, the character will be unbalanced or will 
leave his guard open and, until his next combat round 
starts, all attacks made against him will be made with 
Advantage. In the same way, in the next turn of the PC 
or NPC that has obtained a failure, he will make his rolls 
with Disadvantage.
44


COMBAT EXAMPLE
C
arla, John and Alex play three different characters, 
Clive (a Bulldog), Hugh (a Shadow) and Nitro 
(a Save), who get involved in a fight while trying 
to assault a warehouse occupied by a gang from the 
northern district. The motive for the assault by the 
players is not important for this example, but we could 
assume that the motive is to recover a briefcase with 
confidential documentation, to deliver it to an important 
executive.
However, the players have not been skilled enough to 
go unnoticed, so they will have to face a group of four 
enemies, two junkies and two gangster members (see 
statistics in CHAPTER 7: Enemies and Mercenaries, 
pages 72, 73 and 74).
The combat starts by making an initiative roll for each 
participant. Players will roll 1D20, add the DEX. MOD, 
and get the following scores: Clive = 14 (12 + DEX. MOD, 
2); Hugh = 16 (16 + DEX. MOD, 0); and Nitro = 10 (8 + 
DEX. MOD, 2). The NPCs, managed by the GD will get 
the following scores: Junkie nº1 = 10; Junkie nº2 = 7; 
Gangster nº1 = 15; and Gangster nº2 = 12. 
The order of action, therefore, will be as follows:
1) - Hugh = 16
2) - Gangster nº1 = 15
3) - Clive = 14
4) - Gangster nº2 = 12
CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
5) - Nitro = 10
6) - Junkie nº1 = 10
7) - Junkie nº2 = 7
NOTE: In case a PC and an NPC have the same initiative 
score, the PC will act first.
 
With the order established, the combat will begin, 
starting with Hugh. Before making an attack roll, Hugh 
will move towards a nearby brick column, and take 
cover behind it, offering partial cover (getting a +4 
bonus to DEX). In this example, we will opt to use the 
general cover rules explained on page 43. Sheltered 
behind the column, Hugh will make an attack roll with 
his Automatic Pistol Extreme, in Single Shot mode, on 
Junkie nº1, who is between the distance marked by the 
weapon and the point blank distance. He rolls 1D20 and 
adds his DEX. MOD, 0, getting a 13. Junkie nº1 DEX. is 
8, so Hugh succeeds with his attack roll. Hugh proceeds 
to make a damage roll and rolls 1D6+4, getting a 5 
(1+4). The damage is subtracted from Junkie nº1 HP (7), 
leaving him with only 2.
 
45


CHAPTER 3: COMBAT SYSTEM
Nitro, on his turn, will attack directly without moving 
position, making a firearm attack roll with his 
submachine gun. He will use an Automatic Fire roll, 
firing a burst of 5 bullets, but making the roll with 
Disadvantage. Being able to fire on 5 enemies at the 
same time, he will decide to fire on the Gangster nº2 
and on the Junkie nº1. Note that, although they are close 
to each other, within the range of optimal distance, the 
Gangster nº2 is sheltered behind a partial cover, getting 
a DEF bonus of +4. Nitro must make an attack roll 
higher than 14 to overcome the DEF of Gangster nº2 
(14=10+4) and Junkie nº1 (8). Nitro makes the attack 
roll and rolls 1D20 twice and adds his DEX. MOD. He 
gets a 17 and a 14, having to take the 14 when making 
the roll with Disadvantage. In either case, he gets a 
16 (14+2), exceeding the DEFs of both enemies. Nitro, 
makes a damage roll, and, when shooting in Automatic 
Fire mode will roll 5D4, obtaining 16. Being two targets, 
each one will lose 8 HP. The Junkie nº1 will fall dead, 
while the Gangster nº2 will only keep 2 HP.
Junkie nº1 having been eliminated, Junkie nº2 will take 
his turn. Having two automatic pistols in his inventory, 
he will make a firearm attack roll with two weapons 
against Nitro. Not having the skill Fight with X, for each 
weapon he makes the attack roll, he will make it with 
Disadvantage. The shooting distance is considered 
optimal. With the first automatic pistol he will get 
7 and 9, failing the roll. With the second automatic 
pistol he will get 9 and 12. Nitro DEF is 11 (10 + CON. 
MOD, 10+1), but we should add the armor bonus of the 
armored clothing (+2), getting, therefore, a DEF of 13 
(11+2). With this, Nitro resists Junkie nº1 attack roll and 
will come out unscathed.
Having finished the turn of the Junkie nº2, the combat 
round will be repeated, ignoring the Gangster nº1 and 
the Junkie nº2, as they have been eliminated during 
the combat. Another option, if the GD considers it 
convenient, is that the NPCs, seeing the result of the 
fight, choose to retreat and flee to conserve their 
remaining life, being able to intervene again, later, in 
another future encounter. If the GD chooses this option, 
the NPCs may be considered victorious in the encounter.
Gangster nº1 will start his turn after Hugh. The NPC 
will move to the maximum of his MOV score, reaching 
Clive, and making a melee attack roll, using his mace. 
Gangster nº1 rolls 1D20 and adds his ATK Bonus, +1, 
getting a 15 (14+1). Clive DEX is 10, so he will not be 
able to resist the attack. The Gangster nº1, will make a 
damage roll and throws 1D6+2, getting an 8 (6+2). The 
damage will be subtracted from Clive´s HP (10), leaving 
him with only 2.
Carla, who is playing Clive, should note that, upon 
suffering the maximum damage value associated with 
the damage roll, she will suffer a Stun Shock (page 49), 
and that, on her game turn, she must make a roll of 
1D20 plus her CON. MOD, and pass a DC=14, in order 
to regain control.
Clive, acting in third place, will make a Stun Shock roll. 
Luckily, he has the Fearless skill which will be added to 
his roll to overcome the DC=14. Clive makes the Stun 
Shock roll and rolls 1D20, plus his CON. MOD (0), plus 
his Fearless skill (2) and gets a 14 (12+2). On getting 
a result equal to or greater than the DC, he will come 
out of his state of shock and will be able to act. The 
first thing Clive does is to consume a Docfast Patch and 
rolls 1D4+2, getting a 4 (2+2), stabilizing his HP at 6. 
Then, without moving, he will make an attack roll with 
firearms against the Gangster nº1. Being in front of the 
target, less than two meters away, the distance will be 
Point Blank, having Advantage in the roll. He rolls twice 
1D20 and adds his DEX. MOD, +1, getting 15 in the first 
roll and 12 in the second. Having Advantage he will take 
the first roll and add his DEX. MOD, getting a 16 (15+1). 
The result is higher than the Gangster DEX (10), so he 
succeeds in the roll. Clive makes a damage roll with his 
Thunder Shotgun and rolls 2D6, getting a 10 (4 and 6). 
Subtracting the points of damage from the Gangster nº1 
HP, the target will be left at 0 HP, dying on the spot.
Gangster nº2, will move towards a car to take cover 
behind it, and, get partial cover, and shoot. He will make 
a firearm attack roll on Nitro, using his submachine gun. 
When making his attack roll, he rolls a 1, resulting in a 
critical failure. The gang member, not only loses the 
turn of his roll, but it will also jam his weapon, having to 
make a unholster roll, on his next turn, of DC=17 (15+JV 
= 15+2).

CHAPTER 4: BODY AND MIND
CHAPTER 4: BODY AND MIND
A 
bead of sweat trickled down 
the paramedic's forehead. He 
was cowering, trembling, in a 
corner of the ambulance. Silently 
he watched the assailants search 
the vehicle's crates as they 
shouted at each other.
	
—What a fucking piece of shit! 
—shouted a punk with a purple crest 
on his head—. There's nothing in 
this vehicle!
	
—There's got to be something, 
dammit! 
—said 
a 
man 
with 
a 
stainless steel jaw. He pulled 
one of the drawers and searched 
inside—. Thirsty, blood bags, 
something!
	
—You, you big dummy! —shouted 
a girl with chrome contact lenses 
at the paramedic—. Where the fuck 
is the stuff? And the medical 
supplies?
	
—There's no supplies.
	
—No 
equipment? 
—the 
girl 
pounced on the paramedic, grabbed 
him by the neck and shot him with 
a revolver—. What are you telling 
me, asshole?
	
—We were on our way back to 
the hospital to replenish the 
material consumed during the night 
—answered the paramedic with his 
hands raised.
	
—Fucking shit! —shouted the 
girl.
	
—Lance, 
take 
the 
doctor 
outside, he'll have to make do 
with what's there —said the man 
with the steel jaw—. Dog won't 
last long.
	
—Make 
do 
with...? 
—The 
paramedic was dragged out of the 
ambulance before he could finish 
the sentence.
47


CHAPTER 4: BODY AND MIND
On the avenue, the paramedic 
beheld a panorama of chaos and 
destruction everywhere he looked.
Shattered tents, burning buildings, 
and half a dozen corpses along 
the asphalt. He saw a twisted, 
bloodied body peering through the 
windshield of the ambulance. It 
had to be Edgar, the driver (or 
what was left of him).
	
—Come on, let's go! Don't 
dawdle —Lance pushed the paramedic 
and pulled him toward a dumpster. 
Leaning against the dumpster, he 
saw a muscular man with a severed 
arm and a dirty wound in his side—.
Dog, hang on!
The muscular man looked at the 
paramedic with a smile and coughed.
	
—At last the doctor is here!
	
—You're 
wrong, 
I'm 
not 
a 
doctor, I'm just a simple...!
	
—You're going to operate on 
Dog so, yes, now you're going to 
be the doctor —said the man with 
the steel jaw as he exited the 
ambulance followed by the purple-
crested punk.
	
—But this is crazy! I don't 
have the know-how, I don't have 
the means, no...
	
—That doesn't matter now, you 
have to operate on him. Here, 
you'll be able to stack this —the 
purple-crested punk tossed a bag 
to the paramedic. He caught it on 
the fly, and when he opened it, 
he saw a variety of material, but 
not enough to operate—. But, but, 
but, but with this I can hardly 
operate...
	
—You have a couple of gauze 
pads, forceps, a scalpel, some 
sedatives and a staple gun, enough 
to do the job —said the man with 
the steel jaw.
	
—Yes, but I don't have any 
blood bags to...
	
—Don't worry about that —Lance 
grabbed a broken bottle from the 
floor, pulled a knife from his 
belt and held it in the palm of 
his hand—. My blood type is O 
negative. I have plenty to spare.
	
—What are you trying to do, 
sew his missing arm back on? But 
we don't even know where it is!
	
—As long as you can patch it 
up so it's operational, and we 
can get the hell out of here, doc 
—said the purple-crested punk.
 
The conversation was interrupted 
by the sound of sirens approaching.
	
—Well, enough chitchat! The 
timbers are falling and positions 
must be taken. Doc, let's get to 
work. And don't fuck it up —the 
man with the metal jaw motioned 
for them to move and seek cover. 
Lance, before leaving, handed him 
the broken bottle full of blood 
and covered his palm wound with 
gauze.
 
The paramedic crouched down next 
to the wounded man they called Dog 
and looked at the meager medical 
instruments at his disposal.
 
	
—What a mess we've gotten you 
into —Dog said with a discomposed 
look on his face.
	
—Yeah, real shit —replied the 
frightened paramedic—. Why do 
they call you Dog?
	
—Because when I feel I'm going 
to get screwed, I tend to bite. —
Dog drew a gun with the only hand 
he had left and pointed it at the 
paramedic—. You're not going to 
screw me now?
 
The doctor swallowed and prepared 
to operate.
* V.M.P *
48


DEATH AND HEALING
If a character's HP reaches 0 or less, he will be 
incapacitated and will not be able to act or perform any 
action. If his HP does not exceed 0 again in less than a 
certain number of rounds, the PC will die. In how many 
rounds? In 8 + CON. MOD rounds.
Dying Rounds = 8 Rounds + CON. MOD
A character who rests for 8 hours, will recover the total 
HP. In case of resting for 4 hours, he will recover half of 
the total HP (NOTE: For more information on Rest, see 
CHAPTER 2, Game System, page 35). 
 
Applying a regenerative gel or a Medpack (only once 
per combat) allows you to recover health points (list of 
medical equipment on page 86).
DANGERS AND INSTINCTS
The GD may allow a character to try to avoid or mitigate 
the effects of some poisons, diseases, explosions or 
traps. In such cases an Instincts roll will be made. The 
character will roll 1D20 and add his Instincts bonus (INS) 
and the abilities MOD dictated by the GD (for example, 
CON for poisons, DEX to dodge a trap or move away from 
an explosion, CHA to resist an interrogation, etc.). The 
GD must set the value to overcome in the roll depending 
on the situation and its severity, as well as the potency 
of the poison or how well made the trap is, etc.
STUN SHOCK
During a combat, it is possible that a PC or NPC suffers 
a critical on an attack roll, and therefore suffers the 
maximum damage of the weapon +1.
 
Should this circumstance occur, the PC or NPC who 
receives the critical will suffer a Stun Shock. This 
circumstance would also occur if, after a successful 
attack roll, the maximum dice value associated with 
the weapon being used is obtained on the damage roll.
	
 
What does it mean if a target suffers a Stun Shock? That 
the target that has suffered the shock, when it is his 
turn to attack, will be on the ground concussed by the 
previous attack, and may lose his turn of action. To avoid 
this, he must make a 1D20 roll, add his CON. MOD, and 
exceed or equal a DC=14. If he succeeds in the roll, he 
will recover his turn. 
CHAPTER 4: BODY AND MIND
Otherwise he will lose his turn and must make a new 
attempt on his next turn. NOTE: The PC who makes the 
stun shock roll may add the value of the Fearless skill to 
get a higher score.
DRUGS
Drugs and stimulants used to have a clearly negative 
effect on people, but with the evolution of technology, 
new drugs are beginning to emerge that improve 
performance, endurance and even multiply neuronal 
connections, facilitating creative processes. Still, 
everything has a price and this new generation of drugs 
is no different. In terms of gaming, each drug has two 
values: Toxicity and Addictiveness.
 
Toxicity indicates how dangerous it is to take. It is 
indicated by a DC that marks the difficulty to overcome 
in a CON. test. This test must be repeated each time the 
drug is taken to avoid overdosing.
 
Addictiveness indicates how easy it is to get hooked. 
It is indicated by a die (D4, D6, D8, D10, D12 or D20) 
that must be rolled each time the drug is taken. With 
a result of 1 or 2 an addiction develops. For each extra 
dose taken in a day, the Addictiveness die is reduced by 
one level, thus increasing the risk of becoming addicted.
 
OVERDOSE
When suffering an overdose the character must make 
an Instincts (CON) roll against the toxicity of the drug. 
If successful, he will lose 1D4 points of a abilities of his 
choice between Intelligence and Wisdom permanently, 
thus reflecting the neuronal damage caused by the drugs. 
Failing the test, the PC will die and will have 8 rounds 
+ CON. MOD to be revived. If he is revived in time, he 
will lose the characteristic points indicated above. If the 
character reaches an Intelligence or Wisdom score of 0, 
he will enter a state of permanent insanity called "Bad 
Trip". Unfortunately the player will lose control of his 
character, which will have to be removed from the game.
Reanimation Time =
8 Rounds + CON. MOD
Drugs are bad traveling companions and overdoses are 
deadly.
49


CHAPTER 4: BODY AND MIND
ADDICTION
When an addiction is acquired, the character will need 
a daily dose of the drug (performing the corresponding 
overdose test). If he doesn't get it, the first day he will 
have a -2 points to all actions and from the second day 
he will perform all actions with Disadvantage because 
of the withdrawal syndrome. A character addicted to a 
drug will have to take twice the dose to get the normal 
effect.
DETOXICATION
To detoxify, a character must remain clean for a 
time determined by the GD and once per session roll 
1D20, add the CON. MOD, and pass or equal a DC=16. 
Three successful tests will be necessary to detoxify 
completely. Failure in one of the tests will reset the 
counter to zero and the player must repeat the tests 
until three consecutive successes are obtained.
 
If the PC gets a critical, he is considered to have passed 
two hit tests. A failure will force the player to make the 
next test with Disadvantage.
 
MENTAL HEALTH
Any professional raider worth his salt will encounter 
high-stress situations, massacres, dismembered friends 
and experiments that can turn your insides inside out. 
More than a few end up succumbing to the madness. 
In fact, encountering mad scientists or neocultures 
with insane priests is one of the most typical clichés of 
cyberpunk stories.
 
It will be the character's WIS abilities. that will measure 
his mental health.
 
A character with a value of 3 will be barely lucid, will 
confuse reality with fantasy and will be on the verge of 
absolute madness. Characters that fall below this value 
will go completely insane and will become NPCs as long 
as their WIS value does not change.
 
When a character witnesses some unspeakable horror 
or makes use of the neural connection and gets fried 
due to an encounter against a Black Ice program, he/she 
must make a 1D20 roll and add the WIS. MOD. The GD 
will set the difficulty depending on the severity of the 
horror witnessed or the DC of the Constellation Node 
(the power of the Black Ice).
 
If the PC fails the roll, he will lose 1D6 points of WIS 
temporarily. If the result is a 6, he will lose 1 point of WIS 
permanently. Temporary points are regained at a rate of 
1 point per full day of Rest.
