CHAPTER 5: IMPLANTS
CHAPTER 5: IMPLANTS
T
he young woman walked in front 
of the display and browsed 
through the various implants 
she found: mechanical corneas, 
chrome limbs, synthetic hair, 
lung capacity enhancements... all 
very cool, yes, but nothing earth-
shattering. 
	
—Do you see anything that 
interests you, baby? —asked the 
surgeon behind the counter. The 
gown he was wearing was freshly 
laundered, but traces of blood 
were still visible on the fabric.
	
—I'm still thinking about it 
—said the girl as she walked past 
some chrome sample legs. They look 
very nice.
	
—Interested? They're hardly 
used —said the surgeon proudly. I 
think they're barely three months 
old.
	
—Three months old?
	
—Yes, 
the 
previous 
owner 
acquired them shortly before she 
was hit by a car one night while 
out partying.
	
—Ack —said the girl with a 
disgusted gesture.
	
—The 
blood 
stains 
were 
completely cleaned, I swear —said 
the surgeon.
The young woman turned away from 
the counter and walked over to a 
turntable, where her boyfriend was 
waiting, amused at the turntable 
and the needle of the equipment.
 
	
—Mike, what did you bring me 
to this dump for?
	
—I heard you —muttered the 
surgeon.
	
—To get your first implant, 
honey. It's your birthday and 
it's time to celebrate!
51


CHAPTER 5: IMPLANTS
	 —Is it really?	
	
—Of course it is! It's on me!
	
—But everything here is crap. 
All old and second hand... and 
possibly stolen. And we don't 
even know if it works!
	
—Just think, we're giving a 
second life to still-functional 
implants. 
We're 
helping 
the 
planet!
	
—It's even possible that I'll 
catch an infection and get a 
stroke! —complained the girl.
	
—Don't be melodramatic.
	
—You don't love me either, you 
took me to a second-hand implant 
store?
	
—Don't be manipulative! New 
implants are expensive and my 
salary doesn't give me much joy.
 
The bell at the entrance jingled 
and the door opened. Three men 
in black trench coats entered and 
approached the counter.
 
	
—Romero, you old dog, you look 
great! —said one of the men in 
the black trench coats wearing 
sunglasses.
	
—Ralph, Tyger and Isaac! —
greeted the surgeon—. It's been a 
long time!
	
—It sure has! —said Tyger, a 
tall man with blond hair.
	
—I can see life is going well 
for you, you're getting a belly —
said Isaac, a little shorter but 
bigger than Tyger.
	
—I don't have to be in the front 
line anymore, so I've relaxed a bit 
—said Romero, while patting his 
belly under his surgeon's gown—. 
This is not a courtesy visit, is 
it?
	
—We need implants for a special 
job —answered Ralph.
	
—The good kind, from your 
private collection —added Tyger.
	
—The 
ones 
in 
my 
private 
collection? 
—Romero 
asked—. 
They're not the kind of implants 
you can find on the street, you 
know?
	
—If it's about price, don't 
worry —said Isaac. He pulled a 
card out of his pocket and held 
it out to Romero—. Our bosses are 
picking up the tab.
 
Romero looked at the card carefully 
and then laughed.
 
	
—So, playing with the big 
boys, huh? —Romero handed the card 
back, leaned over the counter and 
pressed a hidden button. The walls 
of the store folded sideways and 
a dozen hidden panels emerged. 
On them rested several implants 
of military manufacture, rarely 
displayed in a store.
The pair of young boys stared at 
the scene in silence and surprise.
 
	
—Hey, why didn't you tell us 
you had more implants behind the 
counters? —asked the girl.
	
—Because these implants are 
for VIP customers. That said, 
your time in the store is over.
	
—But...
	
—I have important clients to 
attend to. Now, go away!
  
 
* V.M.P *
52


I
mplants are enhancements that are surgically 
installed on characters and gradually turn them into 
increasingly synthetic-like beings. They improve 
Abilities modifiers, allow direct access to the Net via a 
neural connection, or give an individual with cybernetic 
eyes the ability to see in the dark (see the complete list 
of Implants on page 54).
When a PC wants to have an implant installed, he/she 
must go to a clinic, choose the implant and pay the price. 
The price of the implant includes the graft operation. In 
case the PC brings the implant to the clinic, the price of 
the operation will be 10% of the value of the implant.
 
How does the operation work? At the time of the 
operation, the PC must make a roll of 1D20, plus the 
CON. MOD, and pass an Implant Operation roll (IOP) 
associated with the implant. If the roll is successful, 
the implant will have been successfully grafted. In case 
of failure of the roll, the operation will not have been 
successful, so you will have to wait 8 hours to make a 
new operation attempt. Warning! In case of failure of 
the operation, if applicable, you will have to pay again 
for the operation. If the implant was purchased in the 
same clinic, there will be no cost to repeat the operation.
And what happens in case of a Critical Failure Operation 
roll? In that case, in addition to failing the operation 
to graft the implant, the implant will be defective and 
unusable.
 
As stated above, with each grafted implant, you will lose 
Soul points (which will vary depending on the implant), 
plus there is a chance of acquiring Synthetic Tares. 
This will require a Soul Connection (SCO) roll, after a 
successful operation grafting the implant. The SCO will 
indicate what will be the DC of the roll to overcome 
and the MOD of the associated ability according to 
the implant. With the roll, there will be four possible 
situations:
•	 If a critical success is obtained in the SCO roll, no 
Soul point will be lost.
•	 If a success on the SCO roll is obtained, only the 
Soul point associated with the implant will be lost.
•	 A failure on the SCO roll will result in the loss of 
twice the Soul points associated with the implant.
•	 If you get a failure in the SCO roll, in addition to 
losing twice the Soul points associated to the 
implant, you will acquire a random Synthetic Tare. 
CHAPTER 5: IMPLANTS
If in subsequent operations to graft more implants, 
he gets several failures, and acquires all the tares, 
he will not be able to install more implants, even if 
he still has Soul points.
CLINICAL TYPES
One thing to take into account for the PC, before 
grafting an implant, is that depending on the clinic he/
she visits, the operation may be more or less successful. 
It is not the same to do it in a quality, prestigious and 
well-known clinic than in an illegal hovel.
 
There will be two possible options:
• If the PC buys the implant in the store, depending 
on the clinical type, a different percentage will be 
applied on the price of the implant.
• If the PC provides the implant, when performing 
the grafting operation, he will pay 10% of the value 
of the implant for the operation, and according 
to the clinical type, a different percentage will be 
applied on the price of the implant.
What is involved in having the operation performed at 
one clinic or another?
 
Depending on the level of the clinic, a bonus will be 
added or subtracted to the IOP roll (NOTE: This bonus 
will not affect the subsequent SCO roll).
 
For example, if a PC decides to have a Nevsko Sensory 
Amplifier implanted and goes to a Standard Clinic, he 
must pay 3.000 credits, a cost of 15% of the price of 
the implant (450 credits) and make a 1D20 roll plus 
his CON. MOD for a DC=14. To the roll he will add the 
bonus of the Operation associated to the clinic, which 
in this case will be 0. If he had gone to an Illegal Slum, 
the price of the operation would be 5% (150 credits) and 
the bonus of the Operation would be -2, leaving the roll 
as follows:
IOP Roll = 1D20 + CON. MOD
+ Bon. Operation according to Clinic
CAN IMPLANTS BE REMOVED?
Yes, by going to a clinic and paying the price associated 
with the implant to be removed. The extracted implant 
will recover the Alma points spent on it.

WHEN WILL IT NO LONGER BE POSSIBLE 
TO GRAFT IMPLANTS?
•	 When all Soul points have been spent.
•	 When all Synthetic tares have been acquired.
If a PC decides to remove an implant to recover Soul 
points, if he has already acquired all the Synthetic Tares, 
he will not recover Soul points. No matter how many 
implants are removed, the Soul points will remain at 0, 
with all the defects, without implants, and without the 
possibility of grafting new implants.
ABANDOWARE
This type of old-fashioned implants cost half as 
much as the latest generation, but offer worse results. 
The difficulty to repair and maintain these implants 
increases by +2 points.
IMPLANT MAINTENANCE
At the end of each mission, characters must perform 
maintenance work on their implants or risk failure at the 
worst possible moment. How to perform maintenance 
work?
•	 Make an INT roll to pass a maintenance roll: 
Each implant has an associated maintenance 
DC. Failing to maintain an implant will cause you 
to lose the benefits it grants until it is repaired. 
A second attempt at the maintenance roll can 
be made, but this time with Disadvantage. In 
case of failure again, the player must go to an 
implant store to repair the defective implant. If 
the implant to be serviced is of the Abandonware 
type, he must increase the DC by 2 points. 
•	 Go to an implant store: Each implant has a 
maintenance price associated with it, which 
will be the price the player must pay to have it 
functional and ready. If the player has previously 
failed the maintenance roll, the cost of the repair 
will increase according to the type of implant.

COGNIPORT™
It is a technology that makes it possible to load knowledge 
instantly by inserting memory cards into a port installed 
in the back of the neck. There is an endless amount 
of pre-loaded knowledge in the so-called CogniCards, 
which replace the knowledge acquired in the usual 
way temporarily. The player can choose which specific 
type of knowledge he wants to load in his memory and 
from that instant, as long as the card is inserted in the 
port, the loaded program will grant the corresponding 
modifier (up to a maximum of +10) to the D20 roll. This 
implant allows only one CogniCard to be inserted. PRICE: 
2.000 credits.  / IOP: DC=13 / SOUL Points: 1 / SCO: INT/
DC=14 / IMPLANT MAINTE.: DC=12 / PRICE EXTRAC.: 
700 credits / PRICE MAINTE.: 900 credits.

arry leaned back in his seat, 
picked up a glazed donut and 
dunked it into his steaming 
cup of coffee. With his free hand 
he grabbed a keyboard and placed 
it on his belly, while with the 
other he brought the doughnut to 
his lips and took a loud bite. The 
row of green phosphor monitors 
in front of him on the metal 
table sputtered as he savored the 
doughnut with pleasure.
A command line appeared on one of 
the top monitors.
	
—That's odd —Larry muttered. 
He put on the glasses on the table 
and put on the headset with the 
built-in microphone—. Joe, can 
you hear me? Are you there?
	
—Yeah, I'm here —replied a 
voice through the headset—. What's 
up, buddy?
	
—Did you see that command line 
that popped up in the system? It's 
in the constellation of nodes in 
the north building 07. Did you 
write it? —asked Larry-. Someone 
from the morning shift? Or maybe 
it's an update from headquarters?
— Did I enter that? No way. Maybe 
it's from headquarters.
One of the monitors flickered and 
then suddenly shut off.
 
	
-One of the monitors went out, 
the signal failed —said Larry.
	
-You didn't pull one of the 
wires under the table? —Joe asked.

CHAPTER 6: NET, HACKING AND NEW TECHNOLOGIES
NODE CONSTELLATION
It is the virtual representation of an administrative 
entity on the Net. They usually have shapes reminiscent 
of constellations of stars and, like these, have connection 
points known as "nodes" that store information, personal 
data, bank details, business projects, etcetera. To access 
them it is necessary to pass a Cyber skill test with a DC 
according to your level. Once a Node is controlled, it is 
not necessary to make any other control test during that 
incursion. If incursions are made later, you will have to 
take control of the Nodes again.
Nodes have three characteristics:
•	 Difficulty: Number that must be equaled or 
exceeded in a Cyber skill test to access the node.
•	 Alert Level: Dice (D2, D4, D6 or D8) to be rolled 
if the node access roll is not passed. If a 1 or 2 is 
rolled, the intrusion has been detected and the 
countermeasures are triggered; otherwise there 
will be no detection, but the alert level increases 
(the immediately smaller die is used in the 
following occasions).
•	 Countermeasures: Indicates the action taken by 
the node when an intrusion is detected. 
o	 Alert: The node administrators are alerted 
of the intrusion.
o	 Lockout: Access to the node is completely 
closed and the PC cannot access it again. He 
will have to find a new way.
o	 Quarantine: The PC consciousness will be 
trapped in the node awaiting location and 
capture by law enforcement or corporate 
forces. A Cyber skill test with the DC indicated 
by the node's difficulty level must be passed 
to escape quarantine. The Quarantine 
program only affects Cypher who access the 
Net through a neural implant
o	 Black Ice: The node uses programs to search 
for and eliminate intruders. When under 
attack by one of them, the character is 
entitled to make a WIS Instincts roll against 
the node's difficulty. If the test is passed he 
loses 1D6 points of a ability of his choice, 
between INT or WIS. Failing the test, in 
addition to the above, the character will 
suffer a cardiac arrest, having 8 rounds + 
INT. MOD to be revived. If he is not revived 
before the established rounds have elapsed, 
the character will die. In case the character is 
operating from a hacking terminal, the Black 
T
his chapter will describe how the Net works and 
its game mechanics: how to access it and what the 
Node Constellations are, as well as the structures 
of the Nodes and the defensive programs that integrate 
them. 
ACCESSING THE NET
To be able to access the Net and carry out illicit 
activities with a minimum of guarantees, it is necessary 
to have a neural implant or a hacking terminal (see 
Equipment on page 63) with which to connect.
•	 Basic Terminal: With a basic terminal, access 
to the net is limited. You cannot use the help 
programs or install enhancement programs. Such 
terminals are public access terminals designed to 
perform everyday operations, such as accessing 
virtual libraries, booking airline flights or watching 
videos on VirtualTube, and lack the power and 
software needed to break through the defenses of 
a Corporate Node Constellation and have no slots 
to install upgrade programs. All attempts to hack 
into the net are done with Disadvantage.
•	 Hacking Terminal: It has slots to install programs 
and use them at the same time. Attempts to 
hack the net are performed without penalty or 
bonus. These are terminals for private use, are not 
publicly accessible and must be purchased on the 
black market.
•	 Neural Implant: Acts as a physical terminal. Various 
programs can be installed and used, allowing the 
user to see a recreation of a terminal in the palm 
of their hand or visualize it directly on the retina 
of their eyes, the visual effects of which are at the 
discretion of the GD. A neural implant grants a +2 
to Cyber rolls to access the Nodes.
HOW DO YOU MAKE A CYBER ROLL?
The roll, simply, will be resolved by a 1D20 roll, the INT. 
MOD will be added to it, and the value of the Cyber skill 
(adding accessory bonuses in case they exist), and it must 
exceed a DC assigned by the GD or that will depend on the 
DC associated to the Node Constellation to be assaulted. 
 
Cyber roll = 1D20 + INT. MOD + Cyber Skill
vs
GD's DC or Node Constellation's DC.
60


Ice will overload the power supply of the 
terminal causing an identical effect on the 
hacker.
There are 4 node levels with different difficulties, alert 
levels and countermeasures (although the GD can create 
his own custom Constellations and Nodes).
SECRET DOORS AND HIDDEN LINKS
When access is gained to a node, all of its available 
non-hidden links are automatically discovered, thus 
enabling movement from node to node.
 
The programmers who created the Net and the 
Constellations of nodes believed that it would be very 
useful for them to have direct access to the highest levels 
of every corporation, agency, ministry or government 
organization. These secret doors are kept hidden from 
everyone, but those with the right skills can intuit where 
they are located.
 
PCs who wish to detect the hidden doors and links (only 
of those nodes they already control) must pass a second 
Cyber test against the node's DC. 
 
PROGRAMS
Thanks to the programs listed here, users entering the 
Net to hack into a Node Constellation will increase their 
chances of success. The number of programs that can be 
used simultaneously depends on the hacking terminal 
or neural implant where they are installed (see the list 
of Implants on page 54 and the list of Hacking Terminals 
on page 63).
BACKDOOR
Thanks to this program it is possible to increase the alert 
die of a node. Thus a 1D4 Alert Level becomes 1D6 and 
so on (increasing its alert die by one level). PRICE: 1.500 
credits.

CHAPTER 6: NET, HACKING AND NEW TECHNOLOGIES
HACKING EXAMPLE
CøldIce is a Cypher att empti ng to illegally access the 
CyberTech node constellati on. His goal is to control one 
of the low-level nodes with the intenti on of shutti  ng 
down the power to a corporate headquarters, where he 
and his companions want to gain access to "pick up a 
package".
The fi rst node CøldIce connects to is Cybertech's online 
store, which allows public access to the Constellati on. 
The Cypher's neural implant has the Exploit program 
installed that allows him to control that fi rst level 0 node 
without the need for any die roll.
Once controlled, the GD informs him that this node is 
connected to a level 2 Firewall. The Cypher decides 
to check if there is any other hidden link; to do so he 
performs a Cyber skill test against the node's DC=11. 
The player rolls 1D20 and rolls an 11, which added to 
the +2 granted by his neural implant, gives a total of 13. 
CøldIce passes the test and the GD ensures that there 
are no hidden links.
CøldIce then decides to access the next node, the level 2 
Firewall. To control it he rolls 2D20 (Advantage granted 
by his Plugin talent, since it is the fi rst ti me he accesses 
this node) and gets a 9 and a 16. He chooses 16, which 
added to the +2 granted by his neural implant, gives a 
total of 18. The result exceeds the DC=17 of the node, so 
the hacker manages to control it.
Once checked, the GD comments that a link 
communicates with a new level 1 node. Again CøldIce 
decides to check if there is a hidden link; to do so he 
makes a Cyber skill test against the node's DC=17. The 
player rolls 1D20 and gets a 19, which added to the +2 
granted by his neural implant, gives a total of 21. CøldIce 
passes the test and the GD informs him that there is a 
hidden link, leading him to the level 1 server or supply 
node, the target of his raid.
CøldIce, through the hidden link, accesses the supply 
node and att empts to control it through a Cyber test. 
Again he rolls 2D20 (Advantage granted by his Plugin 
talent, as this is the fi rst ti me he accesses this node) and 
gets a 1 and an 8. He chooses the 8, which added to 
the +2 granted by his neural implant, gives a total of 10. 
The result is not enough to overcome the node's DC=14, 
so the Cypher fails to control it and must roll the alert 
die (D6). He rolls a 5, so the countermeasures are not 
triggered, although the alert die drops from D6 to D4. If 
he had rolled 1 or 2, the countermeasures would have 
been triggered (see Countermeasures on page 60).
He decides to risk again, since taking control of the node 
is very important for the mission. He rolls 1D20 (he has 
no Advantage, since it is not the fi rst ti me he accesses 
this node) and gets a 17, which added to the +2 granted 
by his neural implant, gives a total of 19. The result is 
enough to overcome the DC=14, so CøldIce manages 
to control the node and executi ng a very basic script 
manages to stop the power supply.

f the GD prefers to speed up the hacking process

It will no longer be necessary to access, or go through 
the data constellations or use different types of 
hacking programs. The reason? The hacking roll will 
vary, and two values not included in the original 
system will have to be taken into account: the 
Technological Level of the hacking terminal of the PC 
and the Program Strength (STR) he is using.
 
When a PC wants to hack a computer, database, 
take control of a security system, assault a corporate 
server, etc, he must make a hacking roll, consisting 
of rolling 1D20, adding his INT. MOD, his Cyber skill, 
the value of the Technological Level of the hacking 
terminal he is using, and the Program Strength (STR) 
value of the program he is running on the terminal.
1D20 + INT. MOD + Cyber Skill
+ Tech. Level Terminal Hacking + Program STR.
vs
DC Terminal/System/Database to be hacked 
The value of the roll must exceed a DC assigned by 
the GD, according to the importance of the terminal 
or database to be hacked. If the PCs roll is successful, 
he will be able to access the terminal and its data. 
In case of failure, the PC will not be able to hack the 
terminal and access the data, and he will also lose 
the hacking program he is using.
 
If the PC, in addition to failing, obtains a failure in the 
roll, he will suffer one of the following penalties from 
the attached table.
 
Taking into account these differences with respect to 
the original hacking rules, the GD will employ specific 
hacking terminals and programs, included below, 
which he will introduce in the games for use with the 
Alternative Hacking system.
 
NOTE ON DIFFICULTY: Taking into account the amount 
of bonuses that the PC will add in the Hacking roll, 
(INT. MOD, Cyber Skill, Technological Level of the 
hacking terminal and Program Strength (STR)) he will 
have to award higher DC than those that would be 
used in the original system, to set challenging tests 
and prevent them from becoming a mere formality 
because they are too simple.
 
NOTE ON PROGRAMS: The hacking programs will not 
take up space in the terminal, but will be carried by 
the PC in his inventory.
 
NOTE ON CRITICAL SUCCESS: Whenever a 20 is 
obtained in the hacking roll, the hacking will be 
considered successful and the PC will have access to 
the database or server to be attacked.
ALTERNATIVE HACKING RULE
1D6
PENALTIES FOR 
FAILURE HACKING
1
The terminal that is attempted to be 
hacked will increase its DC on subsequent 
attempts. Roll 1D6 to determine the DC 
increase value.
2
The 
terminal 
is 
blocked, 
making 
subsequent hacks impossible.
3
The terminal discharges an electric 
shock on the PC, causing 1D6 points of 
damage.
4
The terminal discharges an electric 
shock on the PC, causing 1D6+4 points 
of damage.
5
The hacking attempt has alerted the 
authorities, who are on the scene in 
1D10 minutes. The intervention team 
will be composed of 2 police/security 
guards, and 1 Special Forces operative.
6
The hacking attempt has alerted the 
authorities, arriving in the area in 1D6 
minutes. The intervention team will be 
composed of 3 police/security guards, 2 
Special Forces operatives, 1 mercenary 
and 1 augmented ninja.

CHAPTER 6: NET, HACKING AND NEW TECHNOLOGIES

placed at forbidden access points or passages to

DRONE OPERATION
Drones are programmed to perform some task 
autonomously and obey basic commands from their 
owner. This way of functioning is called Autopilot.
 
But it is also possible, for some individuals, to connect 
to a drone and operate it as if they were merging their 
mind with the machine. To do this they use a BCI (Brain 
Computer Interface), a helmet or implant with neural 
signal sensors that transmits electrical brain activity 
to the drone, transforming the Output's thoughts into 
action orders. Its price is 1.000 credits.
•	 Autopilot: The quality of task performance 
depends exclusively on the loaded protocols. 
Except in the case of combat (where there are 
features to make the necessary rolls), a drone is 
considered capable of performing correctly and in 
optimal time any simple task for which it has the 
appropriate equipment (lifting weight if it has a 
manipulator arm, recording a scene unfolding in 
DRONES
TYPES OF DRONES
Although there are many different types of drones, and 
each Output loves to make his personal creations, three 
main categories can be identified that serve as the basis 
for most of them.
•	 Insectoid Drone: Small, fragile and easily 
concealable. It is usually used for espionage and 
usually mounts some kind of sensor (audio, video, 
temperature...). It does not carry weapons or 
armor, in order to reduce its size and visibility.
•	 Herbivore Drone: The size of a cat or dog. It can 
mount a weapon and some additional armor. 
It is the most versatile drone model in terms of 
functionalities. They are also called "lapdog", 
because it is the type of drone that an Output 
keeps close to him.
•	 Predator Drone:  Dense armor and the ability to 
mount multiple weapons. It has little mobility, but 
is a turret with legs and target-locating sensors. Its 
HACKING TERMINALS:
•	 Source-1999 Terminal: Created by a former 
employee of a computer security corporation. 
Generic and cheap, but functional and reliable at 
the same time. Technological Level: +2 / PRICE: 
1.700 credits.
•	 Tanaka-2K20 
Terminal: 
Japanese 
terminal 
created by Tanaka Corporation. Commonly used 
by corporate agents and network specialists. 
Technological Level: +3 / PRICE: 2.000 credits.
•	 Edelwise 1880 Terminal: Terminal of European 
origin, created by a group of hackers based in 
the Netherlands. Technological Level: +4 / PRICE: 
2.700 credits.
•	 Longhorn Black Terminal: Prototype terminal, 
manufactured by an undetermined corporation. It 
appears to be from a project that was dropped and 
had its funding withdrawn. Technological Level: 
+5 / PRICE: 4.200 credits.
•	 XXXYYY Terminal: There is no data on this terminal, 
nor origin, nor manufacturer, nor date, nor 
anything else. Technological Level: +10 / PRICE: 
15.000 credits
HACKING PROGRAMS:
•	 Little Demon: Hacking program commonly used 
by security agencies to test system protection. 
Program Strength (STR): +2 / PRICE: 500 
credits.
•	 Hangover Blast: Program created by a group of 
MIT students, who disappeared under strange 
circumstances shortly thereafter. Program 
Strength (STR): +3 / PRICE: 800 credits.
•	 Speedy Rat: Program created by a group of 
Chinese hackers, while on a pilgrimage through 
the abandoned arcologies of Singapore. 
Program Strength (STR): +4 / PRICE: 1.200 
credits.
•	 Union Jack: Program designed by the British 
secret services to break corporate and 
governmental security measures. Program 
Strength (STR): +5 / PRICE: 1.800 credits.
•	 Black Star: Program of unknown origin, but 
rumored to have been created by the founders 
of the Arpanet project. Program Strength 
(STR): +8 / PRICE: 4.000 credits.
66


front of it if it has a video camera...).
•	 BCI connection: The Output uses its Hardware 
bonus for attacks or other complex actions 
performed by the drone, but not its abilities. 
Instead it adds the bonuses that the drone itself 
may have for Modifications and/or Protocols. In 
combat the Output loses the Agility bonus to his 
own Defense and must choose each turn whether 
he performs an action or the drone performs it.
DRONE CHARACTERISTICS
When creating a drone, either as an adversary or as part 
of an operative's team, you must define the following 
characteristics.
•	 HP: The endurance of the drone to impacts. If it 
reaches 0, the drone is disabled until it is repaired.
•	 Attack and Defense: As with humans, your 
modifier to the attack roll and the base difficulty 
to hit when attacked.
•	 Modifications (Talents): Mechanical enhancement 
or addition that allows it to perform some task. For 
example, rotors that allow it to fly, a camera that 
records videos, a reinforced arm to carry heavy 
weights...
•	 Protocols 
(Implants): 
They 
are 
software 
improvements to the drone that optimize the 
execution of a task or allow advanced use of the 
drone's equipment. For example, intelligent aiming 
system that gives a bonus to Attack, image analysis 
ability to search and identify specific objects, use 
the environment to seek cover if attacked...
•	 Weapons (Quantity, Type, Damage): Types of 
attacks the drone can make and the associated 
damage. For example, a mounted machine gun or 
rocket launcher on surface-to-air defense drones.
•	 Armor: Reinforcement of the exterior plates and 
protection of the internal parts, which increases 
the base HP of the drone and not its DEF as 
happens with humans.
•	 Motion: The meters that can be moved in a turn. A 
drone cannot run, it always moves at its maximum 
possible speed. That means you can't perform a 
dash move during combat to get +2 DEF. Only a 
drone that is capable of reaching an MOV of 20 
will get that bonus.
CHAPTER 6: NET, HACKING AND NEW TECHNOLOGIES
MODIFICATIONS
Although some modifications may be decided to be 
more expensive than others, as a general rule, a standard 
price of 2.500 credits will be established for any of them.
•	 Flying: You can't leave the sticky asphalt behind 
and touch the sky. He does.
•	 Increased Speed: MOVx2, permanent +2 DEF if 
MOV is 20.
•	 Silent movement: Something more than putting 
rubber covers on your arachnid's legs. They give a 
+2 to the DC of Perception checks to detect it.
•	 Hidden cargo compartment: For smuggling and 
transporting small objects. It is isolated from 
weapon or metal detectors.
•	 Double ammunition magazine: For those who 
never have enough.
•	 Sensor port: Choose a type when installing; You 
have to pay for each additional sensor added to the 
port, but additional sensors do not count towards 
the Drone Modifications limit: video camera, 
thermograph, audio recorder, communications 
interference...
•	 Aquatic: To operate on water.
•	 Manipulator Arm: Allows you to load heavy 
objects, up to 200 kg.
BASE CHARACTERISTICS ACCORDING TO DRONE TYPE: 
•	 Insectoid: HP: 6 / ATK: +0 / DEF: 14 / Mods: 3 
/ Protocols: 2 / Weapons: 0 / Armor: 0 / MOV: 
10 / PRICE: 5.000 credits
•	 Herbivore: HP: 8 / ATK: +0 / DEF: 12 / Mods: 3 
/ Protocols: 2 / Weapons: 1 / Armor: 1 / MOV: 
10 / PRICE: 10.000 credits
•	 Predator: HP: 10 / ATK: +1 / DEF: 10 / Mods: 2 
/ Protocols: 2 / Weapons: 2 / Armor: 1 / MOV: 
5 / PRICE: 15.000 credits
NOTE: The number of Mods, Protocols, Weapons 
and Armor indicated are the maximum number 
that can be installed or mounted on that type of 
drone. This means that, for example, if you buy an 
insectoid drone for 10.000 credits it will NOT have 
any Mod, Protocol, etc. mounted. You will have 
to pay extra for each of them you want to install.
67


CHAPTER 6: NET, HACKING AND NEW TECHNOLOGIES
•	 Diagnostic and Monitoring Software: If it is 
reduced to 0 HP, the Hardware test DC to repair it 
is reduced to 12.
•	 OptiCamo 
Light&Shadow™: 
After 
applying 
light LowLED lighting strips to the outer casing 
of the drone, it is able to capture the light that 
surrounds it at all times. The LowLED allows the 
different parts of the drone to be illuminated or 
shaded as necessary, so that it is more difficult to 
see with the naked eye. This type of camouflage 
is not capable of fooling thermal scopes, but the 
negligible heating of the LEDs does not make it 
more detectable either. Increases the difficulty 
of Perception checks to detect a drone equipped 
with this protocol by +2.
•	 Kamikaze: The drone will move towards a target 
and explode upon close proximity, or preferably, 
upon contact. The target is entitled to a DEX INS 
roll to take half damage. The DC is 12 against 
Movement 5 drones, DC 15 if they have Movement 
10 and DC 18 for Movement 20. An Insectoid does 
1D6 Damage, a Herbivore 2D6 and a Predator 
3D6. The drone is useless and irreparable after the 
explosion. It can be upgraded without taking up a 
new protocol slot to add a flat +3 to damage dealt. 
A few extra small explosive charges never hurt 
anyone. Oh wait! That's not what I meant...
WEAPONS
Choose from the weapons indicated in CHAPTER 8: 
Weapons, Armor and Equipment. The price is the same, 
and it also uses the same Damage characteristics and 
shot types. Although a drone that mounts weapons will 
normally carry firearms (including a grenade launcher, 
or flamethrower in the case of some Predators like the 
DRagON™), throwing or melee weapons could also be 
mounted. For example, the MantiCore™ drone throws 
sharp knife blades, and the Ariette™ has a reinforced 
front head that charges like a mace..
ARMOR
It uses the same types as humans, although instead of 
adding to the DEF score, it increases their HP. The price 
of each shield is the same as for humans. Subtract 10 
from the Defense provided by the chosen type of Armor 
(ignoring CON. modifiers) and add the result to the 
Drone's HP.
•	 Connectors and Tools Kit: By itself it does not allow 
the drone to perform mechanical or electronic 
operations. But if an Output is connected through 
its BCI, or an Electromechanical Manipulation 
Protocol is installed, the drone can interact with 
devices of all kinds.
•	 Portable Operating Room: Choose a type of 
medical equipment from CHAPTER 8: Weapons, 
Armor and Equipment, page 86, and the drone can 
carry one application of that medical equipment 
for each Modification slot it occupies. It includes 
the necessary devices to administer it to the target 
autonomously. In the case of the C.R.T. Lifecorp 
equipment autonomously detects if the owner 
of the drone has fallen unconscious, becoming 
a potential stiff. As an example, the HoundDoc™ 
model is used in rescue operations after disasters 
and landslides. Carries three C.R.T. Lifecorps to 
resuscitate unconscious victims, allowing them to 
survive until the rescue team arrives.
PROTOCOLS
As with Modifications, every Protocol is given a standard 
price of 2.000 credits. The operatives are in charge of 
collecting that money before ending up in the morgue.
•	 Intelligent Aiming System (IAS): +1 ATK. Can be 
applied up to 3 times, for a total of +3.
•	 Defensive Self-Protection: The drone can spend a 
turn moving and seeking full cover (if available, at 
the GD discretion) or partial cover. If an upgraded 
version is installed (you have to pay the price 
again, but it doesn't take up additional Protocol 
space), the drone gets a +2 to DEF.
•	 Definition and Search for Objectives: Gives a +1 
to a type of Perception, associated with a type of 
sensor (video, audio, thermographic reading...). It 
can be applied up to 3 times per sensor type, up 
to +3. It allows the drone to perform tests to carry 
out tasks such as identifying a face in a crowd, 
following the sound of a particular engine in traffic, 
finding the exact heat reading of an explosive in a 
warehouse...
•	 Electromechanical Manipulation: The drone can 
use a Connector and Tool Kit (see Modifications) 
for simple actions such as bypassing a simple 
electronic lock, sabotaging a car engine so that 
it does not start, keeping a VTOL in the air in a 
fixed position or interfering with the image of a 
Surveillance Camera.
68


Example: A Bulletproof Vest type armor (Defense 
14+CON) subtract 10 (14 – 10), obtaining 4 HPs for the 
drone.
DRONE REPAIR
If a drone is reduced to 0 HP you must repair it before 
you can use it again. For this you will need to pay a 
Handyman or do it yourself.
 
A Handyman will require a quarter of the total price of 
the drone (adding its upgrades, protocols, etc.) to get it 
fully operational.
 
If you do it yourself, a Hardware roll plus INT. MOD 
against DC=15 is required. If you fail, you'll have to go 
with your tail between your legs to a real Handyman. 
But if you roll a 1 (a Critical Failure), your drone will seem 
to work correctly. But it will fail at the most inopportune 
moment, which will be just the moment the GD decides 
so.
CHANGE IN HANDYMAN TALENT
The Handyman talent should specify that the +2 
Hardware is only for repairs, but that it includes drones 
in addition to cyberimplants. And it can be added to the 
PowerUp effect to automatically repair drones, as well 
as implants.
CHAPTER 6: NET, HACKING AND NEW TECHNOLOGIES
EXAMPLE OF CREATING A DRONE
DRON:
“Activating elimination protocol.”
HP: 12 / ATK: +1 / DEF: 14 / MOV: 12 m / Talents: It 
moves through the air at high speed (Mov: 20 m). / 
TRAC. BON.: +1 / HD of Experience: 1D12 / Implants: 
Intelligent Aiming System. / Weapons: A submachine gun 
mount (1D6, 3D4, 5D4). / Armor: Light Alloy (equivalent 
to Bulletproof Vest = +1 Armor Bonus). 
The steps to follow to create this drone would be the 
following:
•	 We give you the base structure of a Herbivore: 
HP: 8/ ATK: +0/ DEF: 12/ MODs: 3/ Protocols: 2/ 
Weapons: 1/ Armor: 1/ MOV: 10/ Price: 10.000 
credits
•	 We buy him the Armor equivalent to a Bulletproof 
Vest (200 credits, +4 HP) and a Submachine Gun 
(1.250 credits) for his weapons slot. We added 
an Intelligent Aiming System (2.000 credits) (+1 
ATK BONUS), and the Flying (2.500 credits) and 
Increased Speed (2.500 credits, base MOV x2) 
modifications. Since this last Mod gives it a base 
MOV of 20, the drone gains a constant +2 DEF.
Its new features will be:
•	 Herbivore: HP: 8+4/ ATK: 0+1/ DEF: 12+2/ 
Weapons: submachine/ MOV: 20/ Price: 10.000 
credits + 8.450 credits
As you can see, for 18.450 credits we have built exactly 
one surveillance drone. Taking into account that, for 
example, the MU1A cargo drone that appears costs 
20.000 credits, it seems like a fair price for a smaller 
device, but with weapons and more additions.
An MU1A, by the way, can be created as a Predator 
(15.000 credits) with Increased Speed (2.500 credits) 
to keep pace with a human (base MOV 5x2 = 10) and a 
Manipulator Arm to manipulate cargo (2.500 credits). 
In total, a price of 20.000 credits as indicated in the 
equipment list in CHAPTER 8: Weapons, Armor and 
Equipment.

T
he atmosphere was heated in 
the police station. It was 
ten o'clock in the morning 
and three casualties and half a 
dozen wounded among the officers 
had already been announced. The 
policemen were milling around 
asking what had happened and who 
the victims had been.
	
—They told me that Vázquez, 
Apon and Dallas have been killed. 
Is that true? —asked a chubby 
policeman.
	
—Apon and Dallas yes, Vazquez 
was wounded. He's in the port 
hospital, along with Brett, Kane, 
Parker, Morton and Nash —answered 
a red-haired policewoman who was 
putting on her vest.
	
—Hadn't three died?
	
—Yes, the other was Johnson.
	
—Johnson? 
—asked 
a 
young, 
curly-haired 
policeman—. 
He 
hadn't even been on the job for a 
month, what a load of crap!
	
—I'm telling you —said the 
policewoman.
	
—I have been told that they 
were minced, like hamburger meat 
—said 
another 
policeman 
with 
shaved hair—. And those who are 
in the hospital, we'll see how 
they get out of it.
	
—If they get out... —said the 
chubby policeman.
 
An old captain, with a broad back, 
arms like oaks and a protruding 
belly, entered the room. A deputy 
was behind him carrying a bundle 
of leaves.
 
	
—Well, lads, that's enough 
chitchat. As you know, three 
officers are dead and six others 
are seriously wounded. It all

happened 
this 
morning, 
when 
responding to a call of a robbery 
at a medical clinic in the suburbs.
	
—Who was the son of a bitch? 
—asked a black policeman.
	
—Rather, those who —corrected 
the captain —. Antonowsky, do the 
honors.
 
The captain's assistant started 
handing out the sheets to the 
cops.
	
—Minh, 
Walker, 
Ramirez, 
Richter, Edgemar and Quaid are 
the Black Roadhouse gang members 
—said the police captain—. They 
are not averse to any kind of 
crime; robbery, kidnapping, drug 
trafficking, murder... Either on 
their own initiative or by order.
	
—Walker... What do I know that 
name from? —asked a blond-haired 
officer.
	
—Bob 
Walker, 
a 
decorated 
ex-serviceman 
who 
fought 
in 
Afghanistan, 
Lithuania, 
Kyrgyzstan, Laos and a long list 
of other confidential destinations 
in the national interest —said 
the captain—. On his return to 
the country he decided to set up 
a mercenary company on his own, 
which did not go particularly 
well, and now he is the leader of 
this band of criminals.
	
—Wasn't Edgemar that multiple 
murderer up to his eyeballs in 
cyber-implants? —asked the chubby 
cop—. I thought he was in the Port 
Fourchon maximum security prison.
	
—He escaped a year and a 
half ago —replied the captain's 
assistant.
	
—Yes, he blew up the north wing 
of the prison during the escape —
added the red-haired policewoman.
	
—What a beast! —said the chubby 
cop.
	
	
—Minh is a very good hacker —
said the curly-haired young cop.
	
—Do you know her? —asked the 
red-haired cop.
	
—A colleague of mine in the 
computer 
crime 
division 
told 
me that his group spent a year 
chasing her on the net. There was 
no way. Just as they were about to 
hunt her down, Minh executed an 
exploit and several officers were 
left with their brains fried.
	
—What a nasty way to go —said 
the red-haired policewoman.
The captain put his hand to his 
mouth and whistled to attract the 
attention of the officers present.
	
—According to some informants, 
Walker's gang was hired to carry 
out the raid on the clinic, by order 
of a corporation in the downtown 
district. We have some leads, but 
nothing is certain yet —said the 
captain—. We are going to split 
into three groups; Lewis, Faxx, 
Kuzak will go to investigate the 
clinic; Duffy, Delaney and Flint 
will go to the downtown district 
and ask about the corporations; 
and Chet, Poulos and Catzo will 
go to the northern neighborhood 
and ask about the thrift stores, 
bars, and dubious locals. Those 
bastards aren't going to fuck with 
the police and get away with it. 
Just be careful out there. That's 
all there is to it.

OPTIONAL RULE: PROGRESSION
EVOLUTIONARY ENEMY STATISTICS
The enemies listed and described in this chapter are 
intended for confrontations against level 1 PCs. To create 
interesting and challenging encounters, the enemies will 
raise their stat values in line with the PCs they face.
 
In what way?
 
For every 2 levels a PC goes up, the enemy NPC will 
go up a level, both in HP (by casting an Experience HD 
associated with the enemy type), and by adding 1 point 
to his ATK bonus and 1 point to his DEF bonus.
 
For example, if a level 6 PC faces a Luxury Escort, the GD 
must upgrade his stats for the combat to be engaged. 
The Luxury Escort will have to roll 3 times 1D8 (3D8), 
to update his HP, and will add 3 points to his ATK bonus 
and 3 points to his DEF bonus. In this way, enemy NPCs 
will scale the values of their stats, offering a challenge 
in combat.
MERCENARIES
It is possible that certain assignments or missions may 
be a bit more complicated than expected, or that your 
group of PCs may not have enough men-at-arms to be 
able to handle them. In that case, whenever you need 
additional firepower, you can always resort to hiring 
mercenaries to help you during raids.
 
Depending on the veteran, experience and equipment 
of the mercenary, their services will have one price or 
another. There will be two forms of payment:
•	 In advance: The PC or PCs will pay the mercenary 
in advance to hire him, and he will render his 
services during the entire game session.
•	 At the end of the session: The PC or the PCs will 
pay the mercenary at the end of the game session. 
The price will have two parts, a fixed one, which 
will be half of the anticipated price, and a variable

AND WHERE CAN MERCENARIES BE FOUND? 
MERCENARIES CAN BE FOUND?
Anywhere where morality and legality are absent:
• Second-hand or pawnbroker stores.
• Weapons stores.
• Suburban bars or night pubs.
• Underground garages and mechanics.
• Discotheques and night clubs.
• Mortuaries.
• Veterans' conventions.
• Corporate headquarters.
MORALITY AND LOYALTY
The mercenaries work as another PC, but controlled by 
the GD. They will help you in the mission you encounter 
and will fight side by side with the PCs, but will not be 
a being without survival instinct. If things go wrong, he 
will most likely leave you stranded.
 
When will a mercenary abandon an assignment?
CHAPTER 7: ENEMIES AND MERCENARIES
•	 Each time a mercenary suffers a critical on an 
attack roll or the full damage of a damage roll, the 
mercenary will doubt his loyalty.
•	 If a mercenary loses more than half of his HP he 
will also question whether it is worth continuing 
the assignment.
One of the PCs of the group, must try to convince the 
mercenary to stay to finish the contract, by means of 
a CHA ability roll against the mercenary's Loyalty. The 
Loyalty DC to match or beat will vary depending on the 
type of mercenary hired. If the PC succeeds in the roll, 
the mercenary will continue with the group. If not, he 
will leave.
How long does this loyalty last?
• Until he receives another critical on an attack roll 
or the full damage of a damage roll.
•  In case he has lost more than half of his HP, if he 
does not recover more than half of his HP in 1D6 
combat turns.
 
After a combat, if a mercenary has asked for more than 
half of his HP, he may ask for a raise and renegotiate the 
price of his services, even attacking the PCs if they do 
not meet his demands. These situations will be decided 
by the GD as he sees fit.
 
NOTE Nº1 ABOUT MERCENARIES: If the PC making the 
Loyalty roll has the talent Military Leader, he will add 
+2 points to the roll and 1 point for each level above 
the first one. Example: If the player is level 2, the total 
bonus will be +3 (2 + 1).
 
NOTE Nº2 ABOUT MERCENARIES: Mercenaries cannot 
be improved by the PC: neither receive implants, nor 
incorporate new equipment or armor.
OPTIONAL RULE:
MERCENARY HPS EVOLUTION
The available mercenaries are designed for level 1 
players, so if players evolve and improve their stats, they 
will find that the mercenaries will not be at the same 
level. To alleviate this, each mercenary will have an 
associated endurance HD to upgrade their HP, and adapt 
it to the level of the players. If a player, for example, is

CHAPTER 8: WEAPONS, ARMOR AND EQUIPMENT
CHAPTER 8: WEAPONS, ARMOR AND EQUIPMENT
F
inger and Yellow contemplated 
the 
different 
tents 
that 
opened up as they passed by. 
The gnawed and garishly colored 
fabrics 
carried 
the 
posters 
announcing the night's specials. 
Merchants 
from 
all 
over 
the 
country fought fiercely for the 
attention of potential customers, 
throwing offers in the air and 
promoting their exclusive and 
novel products.
	
—I love this atmosphere —
commented a man wearing a leather 
jacket—. 
There's 
something 
electric in the air. Like a 
constant adrenaline rush.
	
—Barney, where do they get all 
this stuff? —Finger asked.
	
—A little bit from here, a 
little bit from there... everywhere 
in general —Barney replied as 
he rolled up the sleeves of his 
leather jacket.
	
—That's a bit of a vague 
answer, don't you think? —Yellow 
replied.
	
—Sometimes it's better not to 
ask where the merchandise comes 
from —replied Barney. He pointed 
to a tent, where about ten assault 
rifles were displayed, and said 
—. That booth, for example, is 
run by a retired ex-military man 
who took a shipment of weapons 
from the army warehouses before 
he left. Latest generation and 
experimental weapons.
	
—He 
wanted 
to 
supplement 
retirement with a good pinch, 
mmm? —commented Finger.
	
—That one —said Barney, pointing 
to a store attached to a truck—. 
It's run by a chemical engineer 
who earns a bonus selling second-
hand or soon-to-be-retired medical

equipment. Equipment stolen from
her company, of course, but what 
the heck, it was going to be 
destroyed anyway!
	
—A recycling-conscious person 
—muttered Yellow.
The three of them walked past 
a rusty dumpster with a sign 
surrounded by neon lights over a 
side entrance. 
	
—"Dr. Steiner, implant surgeon 
and 
mechanic" 
—read 
Finger 
surprised—. Really? A surgeon 
here?
	
—Sure, why not? —Barney asked 
with a laugh—. In the Black Well 
market there are all kinds of 
goods, services or supplies.
	
—But implants? —Yellow asked.
	
—Implants, 
weapons, 
armor, 
ammunition, 
drugs, 
medical 
supplies, 
survival 
equipment, 
vehicles... Whatever you want! In 
the Black Well market, everything 
is 
for 
sale, 
even 
exclusive 
and hard-to-find goods through 
official channels. As long as you 
have enough money.
	
—Exclusive 
goods? 
—Finger 
asked.
	
—Hard to find through...? —
asked Yellow.
	
—Yes, yes, that's what I said. 
But of course, one must be willing 
to pay quite a high price. And 
to pay also to the one who acts 
as a contact with the suppliers 
of such a demanded merchandise, 
mmm....
A huge wad of brimming greenbacks 
appeared in front of Barney's 
nose. Yellow's hand held it.
	
—Do you think that will be 
enough, hmm?
	
—Yes, it might be enough —said 
Barney after taking the wad and 
counting it over—. In principle...
	
—I'm sure we can understand 
each other —said Finger.
	
—Of course! —said Barney. He 
walked over to Finger and Yelloy 
and put his arms around both of 
them—. I just remembered a guy 
who's got some top-notch material 
and he'll be glad to meet you. 
Come on, this way!

REFERENCES MELEE WEAPON 
STATISTICAL ABBREVIATIONS
•	 NDV (Negative Dissimulation Value): 
Modifier that reduces the value of the 
player's roll when facing Dissimulation 
rolls against NPCs.
•	 WDV (Weapon Defense Value): Modifier 
for melee weapons that adds bonuses to 
DEF.
•	 WSV (Weapon Stability Value): How 
many points of stability a weapon has 
before breaking.
•	 WRP: Weapon Repair Price.
•	 SWM:  Small Weapon Melee.
•	 MWM: Medium Weapon Melee.
•	 BWM: Big Weapons Melee.
REFERENCES FIREARMS
STATISTICAL ABBREVIATIONS
•	 SS: Indicates the damage caused by a 
bullet in single shot format.
•	 TRB: Indicates the damage caused by a 
Three Round Burst.
•	 AF: Indicates the damage caused by 
automatic fire (5 bullets per round). 
The damage can be distributed among 
a maximum of five targets close to each 
other.
•	 MAGAZINE: Ammunition capacity of 
the weapon.
•	 RANGE: Indicates the optimum range 
of the weapon. Any distance above the 
optimum range is considered Extreme 
range.
•	 JV (Jammed Value): Modifier that 
increases the difficulty of fixing a 
weapon jam during combat.
•	 NDV (Negative Disguise Value): Modifier 
that reduces the value of the player's roll 
when faced with Disguise roll  against 
NPCs.
•	 WDV (Weapon Defense Value):  Modifier 
for melee weapons that adds bonuses to 
DEF.
•	 SWF: Short Weapons firearms.
•	 MWF: Medium Weapons firearms.
•	 LWF: Long Weapons firearms.
•	 HPF: Heavy Weapons firearms.

PRICE: 100 credits
ARMORS
Armor provides additional protection to the PCs 
and NPCs during combat. As indicated in CHAPTER 
1: Character Creation, the DEF is the sum of the Base 
Defense (10), to which will be added the CON. MOD, 
and the bonuses for Armor and Implants.
 
The Armor Bonus, therefore, is the defensive value that 
each armor has associated with it.
 
If, for example, a PC has a CON. MOD of +1, and no 
implant, the DEF will be 11 (Base Defense 10 + 1). By 
acquiring a bulletproof vest, he will add a +1 armor 
bonus, so he will have a DEF of +12.
 
If in a combat, the PC is attacked but the enemy attack 
roll does not exceed his DEF, he will not succeed in 
hitting him and will not suffer any damage. Otherwise, 
if the enemy succeeds in the attack roll, he will have to 
make a damage roll to determine how many HP the PC 
loses. 
 
When a PC or NPC receives a successful attack roll, the 
Armor he is wearing will lose 1 point of Armor Defense 
Value (ADV). This value represents how many points 
of defense an armor has before it becomes useless. It 
will always lose 1 point for each successful attack roll 
(regardless of the damage it receives). When the ADV 
points reach 0, the armor becomes unusable. It can be 
repaired in an armorer or black market store before this 
situation is reached, for a specific price, according to 
each armor. Each repair will recover the total points lost.

CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: A CYBERPUNK MINI-CAMPAIGN
CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE 
LAND: A CYBERPUNK MINI-CAMPAIGN
INTRODUCTION
S
tarting an adventure in a 
cyberpunk context or universe, 
without previous references 
or information, can be a bit more 
arduous than usual. To avoid this 
situation, in this chapter you 
will find a mini-campaign composed 
of three adventures. The idea is 
to introduce the players in a 
strange, new and exotic context, 
so they will incarnate a group 
of PCs who have suffered a car 
accident and do not know who they 
are or how they got there. The 
different adventures will propose 
diverse 
challenges, 
situations 
and events so that the PCs can put 
into practice what is explained 
in the manual, being able to make 
characteristic 
rolls, 
hacking 
rolls, participate in combats, 
etc. The adventures will have a 
markedly linear structure, opening 
up in options and possibilities 
as the players advance in the 
mini-campaign. The GD must keep 
in mind that this mini-campaign 
works as an introduction, as a way 
to put the rules into practice, 
so the initial approach will be 
drawn in broad strokes, being the 
GD the one who fills in the gaps, 
being able to modify or alter the 
development of the adventures as 
he/she wishes.
Reaching the end of this mini-
campaign 
will 
not 
imply 
the 
conclusion of a closed plot, but 
will serve as a prologue for the 
GD to develop his own cyberpunk 
context. Let's get to work!
90


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M1 - THE AWAKENING
PART 1: THE ACCIDENT
The PCs will wake up at the bottom of a deep ravine. 
When they get up, they will find an overturned, twisted, 
split in two and charred transport vehicle a couple of 
meters in front of them. They will also see debris, 
garbage and the remains of goods belonging to the 
transport vehicle scattered around the area. The ravine 
will be surrounded by steep rocks that rise up to the 
orange sky, limiting the area where the PCs will be able 
to move.
 
As they approach the vehicle, they will see at a glance 
the tire marks drawn on the ground, and following the 
trail, they will be able to see at the top of the ravine the 
guardrail of the road split in two. Attempting to climb 
the ravine and reach the road will be impossible for the 
PCs, and the GD should advise against such action.
 
What if one of the PCs insists on climbing the ravine?
 
In that case, the GD will ask for three DEX rolls, since, 
due to the rough terrain, they will have to pause three 
times, to take a breath, before reaching the top. Each 
roll will have a DC=20, falling all the way down if you fail 
only one roll. The damage per fall will be 3D6.
INTRODUCTION 
This first adventure will serve as an introduction for 
novice players to an introductory mini-campaign, where 
they can put into practice the Nights of Chrome rules. 
The plot premise will place the players in a wasteland, 
a deserted area with extreme conditions, far from 
civilization. When they wake up, they will discover 
that they are the survivors of an automobile accident, 
as they find themselves near a large, overturned and 
charred transport vehicle. The players, amnesiacs, will 
not know why they are there or how they got there. And 
most importantly, they will remember nothing of their 
past. The reason? Simply to introduce the players to the 
adventure, with no restrictions on previous events or 
adventures. A complete tabula rasa.
And what will the players have to do in this introductory 
adventure? Essentially, four things:
•	 Obtain equipment (weapons, armor and some 
tools).
•	 Put the basic rules into practice.
•	 Confront their first enemies (members of the Rusty 
Skull's).
•	 Cross the accident zone, located in a narrow 
canyon, and look for a vehicle that will allow them 
to return to civilization (or to the nearest city).
 
 
MISSION 1 - THE AWAKENING
When you wake up you feel a severe headache and a bitter 
aftertaste in your mouth. The harsh midday sun scorches your 
corneas, so you try to cover it with a bruised hand, with little 
success. In pain, you try to stand up, but lose your balance 
and fall to the ground. You can barely stand up. You grab hold 
of what was once a plastic container, now shattered, and try 
to stand up and take a look around you. Rocks, stones, parched 
bushes, debris and an overturned and charred truck behind you. 
You don't know where you are or what you are doing there. And 
worst of all, you don't know who you are. 
91


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M1 - THE AWAKENING
After searching the corpse and collecting the equipment, 
the PCs will head southeast along the winding rock 
gorge. They will cross under a wide arch of rock, which 
will obscure the path, and may cause them some mishap. 
In the dark, covered area they will find a rattlesnake nest 
(assuming that rattlesnakes have survived in this harsh 
climate and not some other new, mutated species). The 
PCs must make individual INS rolls of WIS (DC=13), to 
become aware of the threat. If the PCs succeed, they 
will notice the wildlife and surround the nest without 
being bitten. Each PC who fails the roll will be bitten by 
the snakes, and suffer 1D6 points of damage.
Leaving the wide rock arch, you will come to a rocky 
plain, wide and clear, but still enclosed by large, rugged 
boulders. Near an area of bushes and debris, you will 
find a sealed container, bearing the Deep Gnosis logo. 
To open it will require a Lock Picking roll, using DEX 
(DC=13). In case of failure, the lock will be broken and 
the container cannot be opened. However, if the PCs 
searched the driver of the crashed vehicle, and found 
the silver key, they will be able to use it to open the 
container without further trouble. Inside, they will find 
two Automatic Pistols (Damage = SS: 1D6; TRB: NA / NA; 
AF: NA / NA) and one Automatic Pistol Plus (Damage = 
SS: 1D6+2; TRB: NA / NA; AF: NA / NA) or an Automatic 
Pistol (Damage = SS: 1D6; TRB: NA / NA; AF: NA / NA;), 
an Automatic Pistol Plus (Damage = SS: 1D6+2; TRB: NA 
/ NA; AF: NA / NA) and a Submachine Gun (SS: 1D6; TRB: 
3D4; AF: 5D4;).
PART 2: THE CAVE
South of the rocky plain, the PCs will see a descending 
embankment, and at the bottom an opening in the rock 
wall. The PCs will find no alternative path, so they must 
go deeper into the cave. To their amazement, they will 
discover that there is a lighting system in the walls and 
ceilings, somewhat rudimentary, but functional. After a 
while of walking, you will find primitive constructions 
made with scraps, remains and offal, giving shape to 
some rooms and rooms, finding, mostly, remains of 
food, waste, old books and used phytosanitary products.
Walking through the caves, they will reach a wide and 
clear area, and, wedged at the back, a large construction, 
similar to a warehouse. If, when approaching the 
entrance, one of the PCs makes a successful Survival 
roll (DC=15), using the WIS abilities, he will discover two 
men inside the warehouse searching some boxes. 
WHAT ARE YOUR OPTIONS?
THE ACCIDENT VEHICLE
As you approach the crashed vehicle, a huge transport 
truck, you will find the remains of the merchandise 
scattered on the ground; computer screens, computer 
equipment, medical supplies, surgical material, etc. On 
one side of the vehicle you will see a huge logo stamped 
on it. Something like a kind of electronic brain, a very 
stylized illustration, in metallic blue. Under the logo, in 
blue letters, there was a text: "Deep Gnosis". 
A few meters away from the vehicle, near the driver's cab, 
the PCs will find three to five medical patient capsules, 
split open, connected to several wired helmets. The GD 
should describe these devices as something like virtual 
reality helmets, but without explaining their function to 
them. If they approach the booth, the PCs will see the 
driver badly injured, writhing in pain. Any questions the 
PCs ask or use of social skills to get some information out 
of him will prove fruitless. The man, moments before 
he dies, will babble disjointed, nonsensical sentences, 
mentioning a surprise attack by desert raiders. He will 
also lament how displeased the corporate executives 
will be at losing their test subjects, moments before 
he dies. If the PCs search the man's pockets they will 
find some ID cards and a pair of Docfast Patches. If any 
of the PCs make a Survival roll (DC=15), using the WIS 
characteristic, to search the corpse, they will find in one 
of the pockets a washer with a silver key.
THE RAVINE
Leaving the vehicle behind, the PCs will follow a winding 
path to the east, surrounded by steep rocks. The path, 
narrow, will not allow the PCs to deviate or get lost. 
During their march, the PCs will continue to find remains 
of the accident, not quite understanding how it could 
have gotten there. Is it possible that there are looters 
in the area? When they reach a bend, and go around 
it, they will descend a small slope. At the bottom, near 
some rocks, you will find the body of a uniformed guard. 
As they approach, they will see the logo of the vehicle on 
his clothes, and can deduce that he belongs to the same 
corporation. Searching the body, the PCs will find three 
melee weapons: two Clubs (damage: 1D4) and a Mace 
(damage: 1D6+2). Near the guard's corpse, they will also 
find two armors: a Bulletproof Vest (Armor Bonus: +1) 
and a Tactical Vest (Armor Bonus: +2).

CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M1 - THE AWAKENING
Dozens of shelves will rise up on either side, att ached 
to the walls, and in several rows down the center of the 
warehouse. Near the back, they will fi nd a rotti  ng desk 
with a map nailed to the wall. If the PCs take a look at 
it, they will see on the map a desert wasteland drawn 
at the bott om, with meandering, unfamiliar paths. This 
area will lead to what looks like a secondary road, near 
a service stati on, close to a highway, the A-111. In the 
center area of the map, on the left , there will be a dot 
marking a town, Woodside. In the upper area of the 
map, you will see how the highway leads north, and 
some arrows, drawn in blood, will mark the name of a 
distant city: Red Rock.
Their disheveled, unkempt and rough appearance will 
not inspire much confi dence. Upon entering, the two 
men, looters really, will raise their weapons and pounce 
on the PCs, starti ng a fi ght. If either of the PCs succeeded 
with the Survival roll, they will add +2 points to each 
of the players' Initi ati ve rolls. Otherwise, they will have 
no bonus. As for the combat, it will be enti rely melee (if 
they did not open the sealed Deep Gnosis container). 
The numerical superiority of the PCs should give them a 
tacti cal advantage over their opponents.
With the enemies eliminated, the PCs may roam the 
warehouse and investi gate it freely. The GD should 
describe the setti  ng as a decaying place, full of waste, 
dirt and fi lth. 
CAVE EXTERIOR
1 – Exit of the Cave // 2 – The Warehouse // 3 – Recreati on Room // 4 – The Workshop // 5 – Gasoline Tanks // 
6 – The Gentleman's Vehicle // 7 – Exit of the Cave and road to the highway
CAVE EXTERIOR
94


Having searched the desk and the map, the PCs will see 
an ice cooler, of the commercial type, next to a wide open 
double door. If they take a peek inside the cooler, they 
will find the corpse of a poor hitchhiker. With a Survival 
roll (DC=10), using the WIS abilities, upon searching the 
body, they will find 3 to 5 magazines of ammunition, GD 
choice, for the weapons the PCs have on them.
 
When exiting through the double door, the PCs will 
find more caves to go through. The path will become 
narrower and more twisted, arriving, after a while of 
walking, to a crack in the path. The PCs will have to make 
a DEX roll (DC=14) to avoid it and continue along the 
path on the other side. In case of failure, they will suffer 
2D6 points of damage when falling through the crack. 
Luckily, if they survive, they will be able to reach the 
other side with "ease", crawling up a sloping path.
Is there any other way to get around the chasm? Sure! If 
the PCs take a look in the cooler, and succeed in making 
a Survival roll (DC=15), using the WIS abilities, they will 
be able to find a rope. With it they will be able to tie a 
knot, throw it across the crevasse, hook it to a ledge, 
and, after tying it to another ledge on their side, cross 
the crevasse over without taking any damage. After 
crossing the crevasse, and walking about ten meters 
through the cave, you will notice that the path begins to 
ascend. After a while of walking, you will see a crack in a 
wall, and the light from outside bathing the walls.
PART 3: THE GENTLEMAN'S CAR
As you leave the cave, you will find that the path runs 
down a winding, twisting, descending slope. Below, at 
the end of the path, there will be an old building, an old 
abandoned car depot. As they approach, the PCs will 
see three dangerous-looking men, Rusty Skull's, talking 
animatedly in front of the main door of the depot. All 
will be carrying firearms and armor. On the other side, a 
couple of meters away, three old empty gas tanks, and 
under a rusty porch, a solitary vehicle.
CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M1 - THE AWAKENING
The GD will point out that they are at the gas station 
marked on the map, which they found in the grotto. The 
most logical choice for the PCs will be to take the vehicle, 
leave the porch and head for the cracked and dusty road 
towards the main highway. No apparent direction, but 
away from the dangers of the desert and wasteland.
 
Approaching the vehicle will require, on the part of each 
of the PCs, a head-to-head Stealth roll, employing the 
DEX abilities, against the Rusty Skull's Tracking Bonus 
with the higher score. If any of the PCs fail the roll, the 
whole group will be discovered and a combat will be 
initiated. Otherwise, they will have successfully passed 
unnoticed and will reach the car. Inside, however, they 
will not find the ignition key. By a successful roll (DC=14) 
of the Hardware skill (DEX. MOD or INT. MOD) they 
will be able to hotwire and start the engine. If the PCs 
succeed, they can easily get out of the porch, head for 
the highway and leave the Rusty Skull's behind.
What happens if you don't manage to hotwire and start 
the car?
 
You will have to look for the keys in the car depot. They 
can either go through the front door, after defeating 
the Rusty Skull's, or, go around the building, and enter 
through an open window in the back. Again, each of the 
PCs must make a Stealth roll, using the DEX abilities, 
against the Rusty Skull's Tracking Bonus with the highest 
score.
 
After going around the building and entering through 
the window, it will be time to search the vehicle depot. 
The building will be divided into three zones:
•	 The warehouse: About twenty old vehicles will 
rest there waiting to be scrapped. A clear corridor 
will cross the area. At the opposite end, there will 
be the main door. The area will be guarded by a 
pair of Rusty Skull's. The PCs will be able to evade 
them by making head-to-head Stealth rolls against 
the Rusty Skull's Tracking Bonus with the higher 
NPCs

Grabbing the key will require sneaking up and 
taking it without making noise, thus requiring 
another Stealth roll against the Rusty Skull's 
Tracking Bonus. If either PCs succeeds, he will 
manage to take the key without making a sound. If 
he fails, the Rusty Skull's will not only wake up, but 
will grab a horn from above the table, press it and 
alert the rest of the Rusty Skull's to come to his aid.
Once the PCs have obtained the key, they must leave

The key will fit easily into the ignition. Maneuvering in 
reverse, the PCs will leave the service area, take the 
secondary road and head towards the A-111 highway. 
Where? That doesn't matter, the important thing is to 
get away from all this barbarity, and they will look for 
answers when they reach somewhere safer.
score. Failure will involve fighting.
•	 The recreation room: A couple of bunk beds, a 
few sofas and a console with a flat screen will be

•	 The Workshop: This room, formerly a changing
