96


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M1 - THE AWAKENING
THE DEEP GNOSIS CORPORATION
T
he Deep Gnosis corporati on is, in essence, a small 
corporati on dedicated to the training of arti fi cial 
intelligences. It was founded in 2035, in Cedar 
City, Utah, by four young and ambiti ous executi ves. 
The idea of the corporati on, in its beginnings, was to 
create Arti fi cial Intelligence models (AIs from now on) 
to use them in engineering work, industrial design, 
and in consumer products. AIs were created based 
on programming stati sti cal and logical variables, and 
introducing millions of databases of text, images, 
videos and audios to train the understanding and logic 
of reasoning models.
The technological and fi nancial eff ort was enormous, but not enough, so it was decided to 
use a technique suggested by one of its engineers, but not yet put into practi ce, due to moral 
disagreements: use human brains, connected by virtual reality machines to the AI models, to 
train them with their experiences, memories and past experiences. The virtual reality of the AIs 
presented fi cti ti ous situati ons and events to the test subjects so that they could confront them 
and act as they morally believed appropriate. The decisions recorded, were subsequently studied 
and analyzed by the AIs, which incorporated the results of their analyzes into their reasoning 
models.
Where did these test subjects come from? Nobody knows. Censored and leaked reports 
indicate that they could be ex-convicts, people from lower social classes, missing war 
veterans... never volunteers or subjects aware of being inside a virtual reality experiment.
All of this informati on, known to the GD, can be entered during the adventure or given to the PCs. 
Of course, with a dropper, litt le by litt le. The PCs will be able to know some informati on (virtual 
reality, the training of AI models), but many others will remain hidden (the origin of Deep Gnosis). 
The idea is for the GD to play with the PCs lack of knowledge of the context in which they fi nd 
themselves, provide some ideas from their past, and feed their paranoia.
Does the Deep Gnosis corporati on matt er anything to the development of this adventure? Not 
in principle, but it could serve as a future adventure hook for the GD, once this mini-campaign is 
over.
97


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M1 - THE AWAKENING
JOHN HAMMER, THE GENTLEMAN
How is it possible that among so much scrap and waste, in the middle of nowhere, there is a new 
vehicle, with a full tank and ready to start?
 
That has an explanation.
 
An explanation that will serve as a basis for the next adventure.
 
John Hammer, a Gentleman of Red Rock, was hired to obtain, by whatever methods he saw fit, 
a commodity from an emerging corporation. Who was the client that commissioned said work? 
What corporation did you steal the merchandise from? What did the merchandise consist of? 
None of these questions are important, in principle. Let's say, simply, that he got the merchandise, 
and he headed to the city of Red Rock to deliver it to the client who had hired him.
The problem came later. At some point on the trip back to Red Rock, John made a stop at the 
wrong gas station, and members of the Rusty Skull's pounced on him. Because? Maybe the Rusty 
Skull's didn't want it to be revealed that the gas station belonged to them, or they simply saw 
something of value and decided to keep it. In any case, John's corpse, dismembered and minced, 
is stored in the trunk of the car, and the merchandise in the back seat.
 
And what is the body doing stored there? Who knows! The Rusty Skull's motives are their own.
 
The PCs, somehow, as they approach the gas station or enter the vehicle depot, will learn about 
John Hammer's encounter with the Rusty Skull's from the latter. However, at no point will it be 
mentioned that the Gentleman's body is stored in the trunk of the car. The PCs should not know 
this detail.
 
This is a surprise.
A surprise that will be resolved in the next adventure.
 
What happens if the PCs try to open the trunk before taking the car? They will not be able to, as 
they require an extremely high Lock Picking roll (DC=20), using the DEX abilities. If you succeed, 
something quite unlikely, the GD will say that the lock is stuck and it will be impossible to pick 
it. The lock, and therefore the trunk, must remain closed until the start of the next adventure, 
where, inopportunely, it will open to the misfortune of the PCs.
98


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M2 - NO GASOLINE
Even if the PCs try to go unnoticed and do not intervene, 
the thieves will point their weapons at them and order 
them to hand over any money they have on them. They 
can try to avoid combat, and not hand over the money, if 
they convince them to leave them alone with a CHA roll 
(DC=17). If you fail, it will be time to fight.
 
After the encounter with the bandits, the local police will 
go to the gas station to investigate what happened. He 
will take statements from witnesses and approach the 
PCs to ask them about their unexpected intervention. 
However, the situation will go wrong when one of the 
police officers decides to verify the license plate of 
the PCs vehicle. The policeman's pocket computer 
will report that the vehicle belongs to a dangerous 
Gentleman with a wanted warrant (issued by some Red 
Rock corporation). The PCs may attempt a CHA test to 
try to convince the cops that they have nothing to do 
with the Gentleman mentioned by the cops, but they 
must pass three attempts in a row (DC=20), with the GD 
simulating with the PCs an intense discussion. Things 
will get even more complicated when one of the police 
officers asks them to open the trunk of the vehicle, since 
inside they will find the body of the wanted Gentleman, 
John Hammer. After this discovery, they will accuse 
the PCs of murder, take them to the Woodside police 
headquarters and put them in the cell waiting for the 
Judge to see them in the morning.
PART 1: REFUELING
In this adventure, the GD must introduce the PCs 
to different mechanics of social interaction, terrain 
reconnaissance and investigation, in addition to 
introducing them to different encounters with enemy 
NPCs. Since the tank of their vehicle will be almost 
empty, the first thing the PCs will have to do when they 
arrive in Woodside is to refuel at a gas station.
 
Doing so will not be excessively complicated, since the 
gas station, owned by the multinational Fast Car, will 
be at the entrance to the town. The problem may occur 
when paying for refueling (worth 50-100 credits). If the 
GD doesn't want to make life difficult for the PCs, he 
could suggest that they search the glove compartment 
of the vehicle, where they will find a wallet with a wad 
of bills (between 500 and 2.000 credits). If, on the other 
hand, he prefers to make things a little difficult for you, 
they must pass a WIS roll (DC = 14) to find the wallet 
when searching the vehicle.
 
With a full tank and some money in their pockets, the 
PCs can stroll around Woodside, catch up on the latest 
news, and buy equipment and supplies. If you ask 
passers-by, they will tell you about several places of 
interest; the Depont Clinic, the Willis Armory, Rogan's 
Store and Joe's Joint.
Yes or yes, once the vehicle's tank is refilled and, 
regardless of whether the PCs have taken a walk around 
Woodside, they will find themselves involved in a brawl. 
A group of thugs, bandits or robbers will attack the gas 
station and steal the money from the cash register. 
MISSION 2 - NO GASOLINE
The headache still persists, and you still don't know who you are 
or what has happened to you, but at least, now, you have a car, 
weapons and you are out of that damned desert. You drive along 
a dark road, barely lit by a row of tall street lamps, with no 
destination on the horizon. The encounter with the desert raiders 
is still on your mind, so you travel in silence. However, after 
a couple of hours of driving, the fuel tank gauge brings you out 
of your reverie. It's time to refuel, so you decide to leave the 
road and approach the nearby town: Woodside.
99


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M2 - NO GASOLINE
POLICE CELLS
The police station cells, located in the basement of the 
building, will have eight cells: four cells on the east side 
and four cells on the west side. The access door to the 
basement will be to the north and near it, there will be a 
desk with a sleepy policeman lying in a chair.
To the PCs surprise, they will not be alone in the 
dungeons, as in the cell opposite they will find a highway 
robber.. The robber, known as Spike, will take advantage 
of the fact that the policeman is asleep to introduce 
himself, explaining that he belongs to a family of highway 
robbers, who follow the philosophy of Robin Hood, "steal 
from the rich, to give to the poor." The PCs can introduce 
themselves, tell them how they woke up in the desert, 
how they escaped from the Rusty Skull's gang and the 
shooting at the gas station. If the PCs tell him that they 
were arrested after the cops found the body of a wanted 
Gentleman, Spike will say that the police are most likely 
cross-checking the information with Red Rock police and 
city corporations, and that they are most likely being 
"requested" to eliminate the PCs in retaliation. Spike will 
then suggest that the best thing the PCs can do is escape 
from the police station, recover the Gentleman's vehicle 
and merchandise, and head towards Red Rock to deliver 
the merchandise to the person who hired John Hammer.
NOTE: The PCs may try to escape from the police 
through combat, but the GD must make it clear to them 
that it will not be easy due to the numerical superiority 
of the police in said encounter (four police officers for 
each PC in the group). If the PCs emerge victorious from 
the combat, the GD must harass and put pressure on the 
PCs by launching more police officers at them, in greater 
numbers and frequency, chasing them throughout 
Woodside, until they are hunted down.
PART 2: WOODSIDE POLICE STATION
Once captured, the PCs will be taken to the Woodside 
central police station, where they will be fingerprinted, 
their weapons and equipment seized, and they will be 
interrogated. There will be no chance of convincing the 
agents that they had nothing to do with the Gentleman's 
death using CHA rolls, so they will be sent to the cells to 
await trial.
NOTE: If the PCs still try to convince the police that they 
have nothing to do with the dealer, if they succeed with 
a CHA roll (DC=13), they will only get an invitation to a 
can of soda to let them consume it while they remain in 
the dungeons.
GAS STATION
1 – Vehicle Entry
2 – Vehicle Exit
3 – Parking
4 – Store
5 – Bathrooms
6 – Warehouse
7 – Gasoline Dispenser
100


At that moment Spike makes them a deal: if they help 
him escape and take him and his family, he promises to 
escort the PCs to the town of Red Rock and protect them 
from other gangs of highway robbers and savages.
HOW TO ESCAPE FROM THE POLICE CELLS?
Two options:
•	 If the PCs have the soda that the police gave 
them during the interrogations, they can devise a 
stratagem: Stir the soda, call the guard behind the 
desk to come to the cell and, when he is in front of 
them, to the other side of the bars. , open the soda 
in front of the officer's face. The guard will retreat 
blindly towards Spike's cell, and Spike will take the 
opportunity to hit him on the head and knock him 
unconscious. With the guard K.O. It will be easy for 
Spike to search his pockets, take the keys to the 
cells and free the PCs.
•	 If the PCs did not get the soda during the 
interrogation, they will have to improvise: If they 
take a look at the cell lock, and succeed in a WIS 
roll (DC=10) they will see that the mechanism 
is quite rudimentary, and they can improvise a 
lockpick with a couple of wires. Where could they 
CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M2 - NO GASOLINE
get the wires? In each of the PCs cell they will 
find a cot, and just below each of them, they can 
easily find some wires, which by putting them all 
together and, with an INT roll (DC=13), they can 
improvise a lockpick. They will have three tries 
to shape the wires before ruining them. With the 
lockpick ready, they will be able to pick the locks of 
each of their cells automatically without requiring 
a DEX roll. Once outside, they can pick Spike's lock 
with the lockpick or search the sleepy cop to get 
the keys to the cells.
What happens if they can't create a lockpick? In that 
case the PCs could call the guard to approach the cells 
and, taking advantage of the fact that he is still asleep, 
attack him through the bars to knock him unconscious 
and steal the cell keys. A STR roll (DC=10) will be enough.
 
Before leaving the cell, they can search the police 
officer's desk, where you will find the key to a cabinet 
taped to the opposite wall. Inside, you will find your 
weapons and equipment. Spike can be equipped with 
the police gun, which will help them in their escape, and 
will be controlled by the GD.
 
Once they are ready, they can go up to the police station.
WOODSIDE: AN ORDINARY CITY
Located in the middle of nowhere, Woodside is a commuter town, a place to refuel, grab a quick bite to eat, and run. 
Founded around a powerful oil company, defunct and missing for decades, it languishes and survives thanks to travelers 
who are forced to take a break along the way. The mayor is Irving Mayor, he is the owner of Dust Post, the huge roadside 
bar located on the city limits, which offers accommodation and parking services for the dozens of road users who stop in 
the city. Mayor is a peculiar guy: a guy with people skills, quite boastful and always willing to please. Evil tongues say that 
he shows no mercy to his enemies and does not hesitate to make them disappear as soon as his power is questioned.
Mayor inherited the Dust Post from Old Ben, who was also mayor of Woodside, and this from Big Joe, the last leader of 
Silver Oil, the oil company that founded the city long ago.
 
Woodside is usually a quiet place, where people go about their business and don't usually ask questions. Crime is controlled 
and there are rarely serious altercations. Maybe some drunken fights and some traffic accidents caused by alcohol, but 
nothing out of the ordinary.
 
However, it is rumored that under the apparent calm that pervades Woodside, organ traffickers do business with 
pharmaceutical corporations in the Dust Post parking lots, with the consent of Irwing Mayor, who takes a small commission. 
But they are just rumors from malicious careerists who are trying to usurp his power…. Or so says the mayor of Woodside.
101


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M2 - NO GASOLINE
DEPONT CLINIC
Depont 
franchise 
clinics 
are 
common throughout the United 
States of America. All patients who 
have standard health insurance 
can access basic medical services 
and prescriptions with a 50% 
discount. What if one does not have 
insurance? Well, you will have to pay 
100% of the prescription.
 
The Woodside clinic is run by a 
doctor whose license was revoked 
in the city of Red Rock because of 
a shady deal with a pharmaceutical 
company and some bribes. Dr. 
Angel, which is what the owner 
of the clinic is called, is polite and 
helpful, but somewhat reserved. He 
doesn't like to attract attention and 
likes to follow the rules. However, it 
is possible that, for the right money, 
or if he is convinced by the PCs with 
a CHA roll (DC=14), he may decide 
to show off his secret stash and 
display the illegal drugs he has in his 
personal pantry.
WILLIS ARMORY
Melee weapons, firearms, explosives 
and ammunition will be the goods 
that the PCs will find who visit the 
store of Darryl Willis, the owner 
of the armory. Willis, a retired 
veteran, worked as a bodyguard 
for executives, movie stars, media 
executives, etc. After working for a 
while for Old Ben, the father of the 
mayor of Woodside, he decided to 
settle in the city and open a gun 
store. The armory's stock is limited 
to short and medium weapons, 
firearms, and all types of melee 
weapons. As for armor, it will have 
exclusively light armor.
JOE'S JOINT
(Clinical Type = Old and under-
resourced clinic).
Joe, a gang member turned good 
Samaritan, decided to open this 
implant clinic when he decided to 
leave his life of crime. He earned 
a degree in nursing from Red 
Rock Night School and a degree 
in cybertechnology from Ponca 
University (ranking last in his class). 
With these two qualifications, he 
obtained the license to open his low-
cost implant surgery business. And 
the rest, as they say, is history.
WOODSIDE FEATURED PLACES
ROGAN'S STORE
Rogan, a former telephone company 
worker, opened this 24-hour store 
for 
one-time 
and 
last-minute 
purchases. Inside, the PCs can find 
various equipment, such as tool 
boxes, flashlights, flare guns, etc. 
If they need hacking programs or 
network terminals, Rogan will agree 
to show them his pirated programs, 
for a small fee (set by the GD), or by 
passing a CHAR roll (DC=14).
NEW ENGINES CORP
(Clinical Type = Standard Clinic).
Widespread franchise of cybernetic 
implants. If you want to play it safe 
and not pay more, it is the best 
option. The establishments are 
austere and impersonal. and the 
treatment with the client is a bit 
cold, but the doctors are competent 
professionals who know how to do 
their job well.
ARES BIO SCULPTURE
(Clinical Type = High-end clinic).
The Ares corporation is one of the 
most select firms in the cyber implant 
sector, guaranteeing efficiency and 
satisfaction in the operations and 
services they offer to their clients. 
The corporation's establishments, 
more similar to beauty clinics or 
spas, radiate luxury and prestige 
as soon as you enter their interior. 
The surgeons look like athletes and 
the assistants like supermodels. 
Beauty will be common among 
the professionals who work in the 
Ares clinics. A somewhat… artificial 
beauty.

CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M2 - NO GASOLINE
WOODSIDE RUMOR TABLE
If the GD wants to introduce the PCs to some side hustle to get them some experience, money, or 
equipment before being arrested by the Woodside police, they can use this rumor table to have an 
adventure hook to present to the players.
1D20
RUMOR
1-2
A group of punks, regulars at the Sands Shopping Center, have robbed a van full of premium quality 
cyberimplants. Maybe it would be a good option to investigate the area and see what is true.
3-4
The local newspaper, News of Tomorrow, located near the Depont clinic, has been attacked by a group 
of hackers who have stolen information from the editor-in-chief's computer. According to what they say, 
evidence of a case of urban corruption by the city council.
5-6
In the Rius Cafeteria, at the east entrance of Woodside, they say they saw a man with a rather creepy 
implant. They say he had a blank stare and seemed quite out of it. He looked dangerous.
7-8
The beggars who lurk near the municipal landfill say they have seen a completely mechanical being 
rummaging through the piles of garbage and waste. According to rumors, he was looking for spare parts to 
replace his old and deteriorating implants.
9-10
At the Woodside police station, on the notice board, there is a wanted poster that stands out above the 
rest: that of an extremely bloodthirsty mercenary and hitman, who has murdered several police officers 
and some important local businessmen. It is unknown who hired him, but he must be someone with 
connections and a lot of money.
11-12
The band Green Onions is on tour, and has a gig at Bar Venture this weekend. Groupies and fans have 
flocked to the doors of the venue to buy advance tickets for the event, but there aren't many left and 
prices have skyrocketed. Someone with good contacts and who knows a well-connected Gentleman could 
surely get tickets at a good price and then resell them. Easy money!
13-14
Police have sealed off the Blue Motel and are questioning the concierge and several of the guests. They say 
a shootout occurred in one of the rooms between two executives from rival corporations. Both lie dead in 
room 301, but it seems that a briefcase with confidential documentation has disappeared. Someone must 
have taken it, but they haven't left any trace.
15-16
There's a pretty nasty guy who is bullying the girls at Club Eros. A bully with cyber augmentations, a bastard 
who thinks he's tougher than he really is, but no one has yet decided to stand up to him. He is usually in 
the club's VIP room. Maybe someone will decide to tell him the truth and explain to him that, without 
manners, life tends to get shorter.
17-18
Daryl Willis, the owner of Willis Gun Shop, is worried about a shipment that has been delayed. His usual 
supplier says that he sent the merchandise a week ago, but Willis assures that that is impossible, that it 
has not arrived to him yet. He suspects that some group of highway robbers must have found the carrier 
carrying the merchandise and decided to steal it. Willis suspects the Dead Vultures, a gang guarding the 
northern road near the old abandoned mine.
19-20
A group of truckers drink and laugh animatedly in the dining room of the Dust Post Inn. They seem happy 
and satisfied. Apparently, in the truck parking area, groups of transporters form and clandestine fights and 
illegal bets take place at midnight. The only rule for combatants is that weapons are prohibited. Everything 
else is allowed.
104


THE POLICE STATION
Once upstairs, the PCs, accompanied by Spike, must go 
to the police station parking lot, retrieve the vehicle and 
escape from the premises. At the players' choice, they 
can take the path they prefer, either facing the police 
officers in their path, or trying to go unnoticed with a 
Stealth roll, using the DEX abilities, against the Tracking 
Bonus of the police officer with the highest score they 
come across in the room or corridor they are trying to 
cross.
 
The PCs will be able to move freely through the building, 
but it will not be necessary to visit all the rooms to reach 
the vehicle. However, they must keep in mind that the 
merchandise stolen by John Hammer will not be in the 
vehicle, but in the seized evidence room, so they must go 
there first to recover the merchandise, before recovering 
the vehicle and escaping from the police station.
CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M2 - NO GASOLINE
The vehicle keys will be in the garage, on a desk, in a small 
booth, although they can also try to bypass the ignition 
under the steering wheel using an INT roll (DC=14). 
The sliding garage door can be opened by activating a 
mechanism in the same box where the vehicle keys rest.
 
Another option, if you prefer, would be to ram the 
vehicle against the sliding door and pull it down. To do 
this, the PC driving the vehicle must succeed on a driving 
roll (DC=17). If you fail, you can try two more times, 
increasing the difficulty one point with each attempt. 
If they fail both times, the vehicle will be disabled, so 
they must steal a police vehicle to escape in the outdoor 
parking lot.
 
Is there a downside to stealing a police vehicle? Let's 
just say, police vehicles are more closely monitored 
than ordinary vehicles and will attract more attention 
when driving on the road. If the PCs decide to take a 
police vehicle, as soon as they leave the police station 
premises, the GD must roll a D20. If the result is equal 
to or less than 5, the PCs will go unnoticed and will be 
able to escape the city without incident. Otherwise, if 
they roll a higher result, the PCs will be spotted by patrol 
cars and a chase will ensue. The GD must roll 1D10 to 
determine how many police cars will intervene, applying 
the chase and escape rules explained in CHAPTER 2: 
Game System, page 36. If the PCs are successful and 
give their pursuers the slip, they will have achieved their 
objective, and they will escape from Woodside.
POLICE CELLS
1 – PCs cells
2 – Spike Cell
3 – Desk of the police officer on duty
4 – Cabinet with weapons and equipment
5 – Stairs to the Ground Floor
POLICE CELLS

1ST FLOOR
Reception Desk (2): Behind the table there will be a 
simple administrator, an unarmed worker who will 
not represent any danger in combat. The PCs will 
be able to pass by without being detected if they 
succeed on a DEX roll (DC=12).
Work area (4): On the east side of the building 
there will be a large work area, with seven desks 
and several police officers working. The GD will 
determine the number of agents with 1D6. The PCs 
will be able to go unnoticed with opposed Stealth 
(DEX) rolls against the police's Tracking Bonuses.
Human Resources (5): A mundane office where 
payroll accounting and overtime for police officers 
are kept. There will be a 50% chance that there is 
an agent inside.
Seized evidence room (6): In this room you will find 
the merchandise seized from John Hammer's car 
and which must be recovered by the PCs to deliver 
to their contact. There will be at least two police 
officers inside.
Forensic (7): Forensic and medical diagnostic room. 
Corpses, in different degrees of decomposition, will 
be common in this room.
Archive (8): Closed or abandoned cases and 
investigations are stored in this room.
Lockers (9): The police station locker room. There is 
a 50% chance there are agents inside. If so, the GD 
will roll 1D10 to determine the number of police 
officers.
Seized Vehicle Garage (10): A large area full of dust 
and grime will contain vehicles seized by the police. 
This time, there will only be John Hammer's vehicle 
parked near the south wall. On the northwest side, 
there will be a small sentry box with a policeman 
inside on guard.
John Hammer's Vehicle (11): John Hammer's 
vehicle that must be used by the PCs to escape 
from the police station.
Guardhouse (12): Inside there will be a small 
desk, with the keys to John Hammer's vehicle in a 
drawer. On one of the walls, there will be a panel 
that controls the garage door for seized vehicles.
2ST FLOOR
Detective offices (2): High-ranking detectives, in 
charge of more complicated or more important 
cases, will work in pairs, in private and personal 
offices. Yes or yes, there will be a minimum of 
one police inspector per office.
Laboratories (3): Room for analysis of genetic 
samples, fingerprints and other biological 
evidence.
Senior officer's office (4): The commanding officer 
of the police station will work in this office. A 
tough guy who will not hesitate to draw his 
weapon as soon as he sees an intruder. The most 
advisable thing will be to go unnoticed.
Communications 
(6): 
Room 
dedicated 
to 
communications and radio transmissions. The 
officers in charge in the room will maintain 
contact with other police stations in nearby cities, 
and will coordinate the Woodside patrol cars to 
report the different crimes and crimes that are 
occurring.
Armory (7): The weapons, ammunition and 
armor used by the police will be stored in this 
warehouse. It will be guarded by four “suspicious” 
police officers on duty.
IT and cybersecurity (8): Room in charge of 
investigating computer and cybersecurity crimes. 
There will be two police inspectors working and 
four surveillance drones guarding the entrance.

CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M3 - THE FAMILY
But, the simplest thing for the PCs will be to accept 
Lurth's proposal so that he and his family agree to 
protect them.
 
Well, actually, in this adventure there will be two possible 
jobs that Lurth can propose to the PCs. The GD must 
choose one of the two and present it to the PCs. Or if he 
prefers, propose both jobs, if he wants the adventure to 
be longer, and changing and adjusting the identity of the 
objectives to be saved (at the GD's discretion), to avoid 
inconsistencies in the plot.
OPTION 1: THE CASINO RAID
THE PROBLEM
Marky, Spike's younger brother, and a big fan of gambling, 
got into serious trouble with Liberty, a loan shark and 
owner of the Tiberius Casino, a famous gambling den in 
Notus City, a shithole a couple of kilometers to the east.
During a poker game, Marky bet more money than he 
had on him, and lost everything in a hand he thought 
he had won. Unable to pay the debts, Liberty decided 
to keep Marky as a trophy, and use him as a fighter in 
his private gladiator pit that he has in the basement of 
the casino.
 
The job that Lurth proposes, given the impossibility of 
paying his son's debt, is simple: that the PCs infiltrate 
the casino, locate Marky and get him out of there alive. 
Once this is done, Lurth will not hesitate to fulfill Spike's 
promise to the PCs, and will escort them to the city of 
Red Rock.
THE FIRE WHEELS
After a long drive along dusty roads, the PCs will be 
led to Spike's family, The Fire Wheels, a gang of highway 
robbers who have a camp in an abandoned highway 
souvenir shop. The leader of the gang, Lurth, will be 
Spike's father, and will be very happy and grateful for 
the return of his son.
After the introductions, Spike will explain to his father 
the promise he made to the PCs for getting him out of 
jail, to escort them down the highway and protect them 
from other gangs of raiders on their trip to Red Rock. 
Although Lurth will nod in agreement after listening to 
his son, he will point out to the PCs that the trip to Red 
Rock will have to wait, as a setback has arisen that must 
be resolved as soon as possible.
This setback will be a mission that the PCs will have to 
intervene, entrusted by Lurth, and that will be of great 
relevance. The PCs may refuse to help him, but they will 
have to march alone, without the protection of The Fire 
Wheels, along highways full of danger and violence. The 
GD must launch gangs of looters to go after them, with 
large displacement cars, and initiate chase events. If 
they fail in their escape, and the PCs must fight on foot, 
the GD must propose combats in which the enemies 
outnumber them 4 to 1. These events must be presented 
and narrated over a period of 2 days of travel. , before 
reaching Red Rock... if the PCs manage to survive the 
fighting.
MISSION 3 - THE FAMILY
Spike, at the wheel of John Hammer's car, cruised the dusty roads 
all night long. Meanwhile, you and your companions remain silent, 
staring at the lights on the horizon. The situation seems calm, 
and the atmosphere in the vehicle seems relaxed, however, you 
are concerned that you will not reach an agreement with Spike's 
family to cover your back on your trip to Red Rock. On the other 
hand, you don't know how the guy who hired John Hammer will 
receive you, nor if he will willingly agree to pay you, complete 
strangers, for the merchandise the late Gentleman got. The future 
is projected black, like a huge and threatening shadow.
111


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M3 - THE FAMILY
If this occurs, 2D10 security employees will come to 
arrest (and eliminate) the PCs. They can also try to go 
unnoticed with an opposed Stealth roll, using the DEX 
abilities, against the receptionist's Tracking Bonus.
Leaving the reception behind, you will reach a large 
gaming area, with several blackjack and roulette tables 
along the 1st floor. In front of them, they will find a 
bustling bar counter, with stairs, on both sides, that 
will go up to the 2nd floor. In the northeast area the 
building will extend, but this area will be restricted to 
casino personnel (dealers, waiters, security members, 
etc.). Access will be protected by four bodyguards. 
Again, the PCs can either bribe them off (1.000 credits) 
or fight them. If the PCs choose the latter option, there 
is a 75% chance that a member of casino security will 
witness it. If this occurs, 2D20 security personnel will 
come to arrest (and eliminate) the PCs. They can also 
attempt to remain unnoticed with an opposed Stealth 
roll, using the DEX abilities, against the Tracking Bonus 
of the security employee with the highest score.
Is there another way to avoid them? The PCs can try to 
impersonate security personnel if they find personnel 
identification badges. Where can they find these passes?
•	 The bartender behind the bar will sell his for 100 
credits.
•  A drunk, in one of the bathrooms, will have it in his 
jacket pocket. The PCs, searching his pockets, will 
find him if they succeed on a WIS roll (DC=14).
• On a shelf, in the storage room behind the bar 
counter, if they locate it with a successful WIS roll 
(DC=11).
• In the west warehouse, next to the desk, where 
a security member is sleeping. A crafty PC who 
succeeds with a DEX roll (DC=11) will get the pass 
without waking Cinderella.
Once they access the area reserved for employees, they 
will be able to find and visit different rooms (lockers, 
meeting room, security room and surveillance cameras). 
If the PCs carry the passes, they will not be attacked or 
bothered. Otherwise, there will be a 75% chance that 
they will encounter a security member in the rooms, 
and they may encounter 1D6 enemies.
THE TIBERIUS CASINO
The PCs will be escorted to Notus City and taken to 
the Tiberius Casino. This mission will be exclusively 
infiltration, with no need to draw or give background to 
the city of Notus City (unless the GD prefers to introduce 
some touches of atmosphere).
 
Before starting the mission, you can talk to The Fire 
Wheels armorer and purchase some equipment, 
weapons or armor (at the GD's discretion). Once this is 
done, Spike, controlled by the GD, will join the rescue 
operation and help the PCs.
HOW TO PROCEED?
The idea, in principle, is to propose three possible entry 
routes:
1.	Through the main door: The PCs will pretend to 
be casino players, they will have to reach the area 
reserved for employees and cross different rooms 
and halls until they find Marky.
2.	Access through the garage door: By going around 
the building, you will be able to enter through 
the employee parking lot door if you pose as 
employees or if you knock out the security guards. 
From there, they will access the area reserved for 
employees, and go through the casino in search of 
Marky.
3.	The route of the sewers: The PCs will have to 
descend through the sewers, go through the 
sewers that cross the casino and access the 
basements of the building. Once inside, they must 
search for Marky.
CASINO 1ST FLOOR
The main entrance will be guarded by two security 
officers, preventing access to the PCs. You will have 
three options to access the interior of the casino: pay 
the entrance fee, 20 credits per head; convince them to 
let them pass for free using a CHA roll (DC=12); or use 
your fists to knock them out.
Once inside, you will arrive at the reception. The girl 
behind the counter will ask the PCs to put down their 
weapons. The girl will accept a bribe (500 credits) or she 
can be eliminated so that she stops bothering. If the PCs 
choose the latter option, there is a 50% chance that a 
member of casino security will witness it. 
112

As for Liberty's offi  ce, a Lock Picking roll (DC=17) will 
also be required to access it, with a 50% chance of 
fi nding Liberty, the owner of the casino, inside. If this is 
the case, combat will ensue. Otherwise, the PCs will be 
able to search the desk, potenti ally fi nding the keys to 
the basement dungeons.
CASINO BASEMENT
Once the PCs reach the basement of the casino, they 
must navigate a grott o, from east to west, guarded by 
patrols of surveillance drones, and check each of the 
four cells they fi nd. By taking a look through the door at 
eye level, you will be able to discover if Marky is in one 
of the cells. Once found, they must remove it using a 
Lock Picking roll (DC=18). If they have obtained the key 
to the cells in Liberty's offi  ce, they will be able to open 
the lock without having to make a Lock Picking roll.
On their way, the PCs can visit other rooms such as 
the security room, the safe, the heati ng room and the 
emergency generator, or the underground coliseum.
In this same area, you will also fi nd access to the 
employee parking, the employee elevator to the 2nd 
fl oor and the stairs to the basement. These stairs will be 
the ones the PCs must reach to reach the basement and 
fi nd Marky.
CASINO 2ST FLOOR
If they wish, the PCs can go up to the 2nd fl oor of the 
casino and take a tour of the gaming tables. In the south 
area, you will fi nd a stage with a jazz group playing, and 
in the northeast area, you will also fi nd another area 
restricted to casino staff . To access it, and leave behind 
the security personnel guarding it (four men), the PCs
must proceed in the same way as explained in the case 
of the 1st fl oor.
In the personnel area, the PCs will be able to visit 
diff erent rooms, highlighti ng the equipment warehouse 
and armory, and Liberty's offi  ce. In the equipment 
warehouse and armory, you must make a Lock Picking 
roll (DC=17) to access the interior. There will be a 50% 
chance of fi nding security personnel inside, 1D6 men. 
The weapons the PCs fi nd in the armory will be chosen 
by the GD at his discreti on.

• In the security room, the PCs will fi nd two security

•  In the underground coliseum, the PCs will fi nd a 
large room with three rows of stands surrounding

•  In the heati ng room and the emergency generator,

HOW CAN THE GD MAKE THINGS MORE DIFFICULT 
FOR THE PCs?
When the PCs reach the basement, and take a look at 
each of the cells, upon reaching the last one, the GD
must tell them that Marky is not there. 
At that moment the GD must make them noti ce that 
a loud noise and commoti on is taking place in the 
underground coliseum. Upon arrival, the PCs will fi nd 
Marky engaged in mortal combat against another 
fi ghter, in the pit in the center. The PCs will have to 
intervene and try to get Marky out of the mess. If they 
decide to fi ght, the GD must face them against several 
gladiators in the pit, and may add several members of 
the security staff  to liven up the party.
THE SEWERS ROUTE
If the PCs prefer to take an alternati ve (and slightly 
dirti er) route to reach Marky, they can choose to enter 
the sewers, and face the dangers that reside there.
The entrance sewer will be a couple of meters from 
the west side of the casino. A STR roll (DC=14) will be 
required to lift  the lid.

knocked down with a STR roll (DC=17). Behind the wall, 
they will find a locker with large caliber weapons, ammo, 
and medkits, at the GDs discretion.
 
In the southern area, the PCs will come across a chamber 
with a huge crack running through it from side to side. 
To cross it, the PCs must make a DEX roll (DC=17). If they 
fail, they will fall into a dark abyss, of indeterminate 
height, and automatically die.
 
After passing through the chamber of the crack in 
the floor, the PCs will reach a dirty and smelly room, 
where Eld the Handsome, a former gladiator from the 
underground coliseum, retired after years of relentless 
fighting, will await the PCs for a last and glorious fight. 
The fight will be inevitable.
 
If they survive, the PCs can continue north, traveling 
through a rocky, damp grotto, until they reach a chamber 
with a metal ladder and a manhole cover on the roof. 
Opening it will require a STR roll (DC=13). Once opened, 
the PCs will reach the emergency generator room in 
the basement of the casino. From there, you can tour 
the basement, inspecting the different cells in search of 
Marky, as explained in the Casino Basement section.
Once opened, you can descend the stairs and reach the 
access chamber. Each time the PCs move to another 
chamber or room, they must roll 1D20. If the result is 
less than 7, they will move without problems. If they roll 
a number equal to or greater than 7, they will encounter 
1D6 killer rats, which they must face.
In the chamber with the two doors, the PCs will 
encounter four punks who have made the sewers their 
home. The GD will decide how the PCs can proceed with 
the punks (persuading them, bribing them, avoiding 
them, or fighting them).
 
If they choose the door on the left, the PCs will reach 
a chamber with an abandoned warehouse, where they 
can find, above all, ammunition for their weapons and 
light armor.
 
If, on the other hand, the PCs choose the door on the 
right, they will reach a chamber flooded with sewage. 
If they decide to cross the flooded area, they must pass 
a CON roll (DC=14) to avoid becoming contaminated. If 
they fail, they will suffer 1D6 points of damage and lose 
2 points of CON, for the remaining time of the game 
session.
Both chambers will lead to another chamber with 
loot hidden behind a false wall. If they pass a WIS roll 
(DC=13) they will detect the false wall, which can be 
116


MARKY HAS BEEN RELEASED. NOW WHAT?
Well, simple: we will have to put land in the middle. With 
Marky free, the PCs will be able to lend him weapons and 
armor to equip, being controlled by the GD, and serving 
as help during the escape. The PCs can choose the route 
that seems best to them, through the sewers, across the 
first floor of the casino or through the employee parking 
lot. The important thing is to leave with your skin intact.
OPTION 2: THE GAS STATION
AND THE CYBERPSYCHOPATH
During Spike's stay in the Woodside cells, Marky, 
his younger brother, decided to organize a rescue plan 
without their father Lurth finding out. To do this, he 
decided to ask for help from some highway robbers, 
known as the Steel Amazons, to try to rescue Spike 
from the Woodside police station. However, Lurth found 
out about this and sent a couple of men to stop him. 
The situation became complicated, since Lurth's men, 
following the trail of Marky, who had requested a 
meeting with the Steel Amazons at an abandoned gas 
station, discovered the place full of corpses, with Marky 
not being among the victims. It is rumored that Marky's 
encounter with the Steel Amazons took place in an area 
near a band of savage, nomadic raiders. The PCs must go 
to the place, find Marky and bring him back.
THE ACTION PLAN
The PCs objective, once they reach the gas station, will 
be simple: discover the hidden lair of the clan of nomadic 
raiders, known as The Marked Ones, find Marky and get 
him out in one piece. To do this, the PCs must navigate 
the tunnel system that they will find behind a secret 
passage, hidden in one of the gas station buildings, and 
make their way facing traps and enemies.
CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: M3 - THE FAMILY
THE ABANDONED GAS STATION
The PCs, upon arriving at the gas station, will find half a 
dozen corpses in the refueling area. They will be horribly 
mutilated, with limbs severed and missing. Without 
having to make any rolls, they will see a trail of blood, 
staining the asphalt, in the direction of a grocery store. 
Inside, you will see the blood stain under a refrigerator. 
With a STR roll (DC=13) it can be moved, revealing a 
secret passage. Before descending, they can search the 
rest of the buildings, being able to find first aid kits in the 
warehouse and ammunition in the garbage container 
near the mechanical car cleaner.
THE CAVE UNDER THE GAS STATION
Once the PCs have descended the passage beneath 
the refrigerator, they will find a convoluted, dark grotto 
filled with biological remains and decomposing bodies. 
The itinerary to find Marky will be full of nooks, traps, 
dangerous places and cannibals ready to carve up the 
PCs, skin them and turn them into pickled meat.
The first room, located at the entrance to the grotto, 
will be clear. Going through the tunnels to the west, the 
PCs will reach a room full of rats (1D10 rats) that will 
confront the PCs. The rats will protect the queen rat, an 
ugly creature with prominent teeth.
From the queen rat's lair, you can take two paths, the 
north grotto or the south grotto. If you take the northern 
grotto, you will find a detour that will take you to a 
chamber with a small altar. If they succeed with a WIS roll 
(DC=14) they will discover a hidden passage behind the 
altar (which will communicate with the kitchen), but to 
open it will require a STR roll (DC=15) to clear the way. If 
they don't notice any of this, the PCs can continue west, 
where they will find a chamber with dozens of bunks. 
This new chamber can also be discovered if the PCs 
ACCESSING THROUGH THE EMPLOYEE PARKING LOT
If the PCs go around the casino, they can find another entrance in the northern area, accessing the employee 
parking lot. The door can be scaled with a DEX roll (DC=14) or a Lock Picking roll  (DC=13). Inside, the PCs 
will find 1D6+2 security employees, grouped into three surveillance patrols. Through opposing Stealth rolls, 
using the DEX abilities, against the Tracking Bonus of the security with the highest score, they will be able 
to go unnoticed. Otherwise, it will be time to fight. Once they have crossed the parking lot, the PCs will 
reach the access door to the area restricted to casino staff. A Lock Picking roll (DC=15) will be required to 
enter. Once inside, since they do not have staff identification passes, they must move carefully so as not to 
be seen.

Marching east, you will reach the kitchen, fi nding 1D6

From the kitchen, heading east, the PCs will have

In the recreati on chamber, combat will be inevitable, as

What will they fi nd in the bunk chamber? Very possibly,

Conti nuing the march towards the north, from the bunk

In essence, rott en meat of unknown origin. The PCs can

THE MARKED ONES
Traveling on roads and highways is often a dangerous 
activity. Highway robbers and wasteland clans are 
commonplace in this ultra-technical, low-morale 
future. However, cannibal clans are less common, 
yes, but much more dangerous. Few come across 
them and live to tell the tale. Among these clans with 
peculiar culinary tastes, The Marked Ones stand out. 
The few reports that there are about this clan speak 
of a kind of warrior society where the fittest fight for 
control of the clan, in combats to the death between 
the aspiring leaders. The winner, in addition to taking 
control and leadership of the clan, has the honor of 
devouring the fallen aspirant to absorb his strength 
and spirit. No one knows for sure how this clan 
arose, but some experts speak of failed, abandoned 
and discarded experiments, of pharmaceutical and 
bioengineering corporations.
 
They do not carry out any economic activity. Only 
assault, loot and murder highway travelers, for later 
skinning, cutting up, dismembering and cooking.
121


CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: EPILOGUE
Gentleman's client, and therefore agree to pay for the 
merchandise.
Will the client accept the PCs treatment easily? If the 
GM wants a quick conclusion to the mini-campaign, he 
could narrate the closing of the deal in a satisfactory way. 
On the other hand, if he prefers to give it a little more 
development, a opposed CHA roll may be necessary to 
convince him to accept the agreement. Or even the GD 
could have the client ask the PCs to perform a small job 
before accepting the deal.
 
Once the delivery of the merchandise has been agreed 
upon and payment has been made, the GD could 
conclude this mini-campaign and this introduction to the 
game system. From here, the GD will have free rein to 
create his own setting or continue with the adventures 
of this mini-campaign, which can be taken as a prologue 
to the rest of the adventure he creates. The GD can use 
John Hammer's client as a provider of assignments or 
jobs for the players, or as a contact or liaison for other 
NPCs interested in acquiring the PCs services.
 
The options are endless.
If the GD has reached this point, he will surely have 
already presented the three adventures that this mini-
campaign consists of to his players and they will have 
completed it with (more or less) success.
WHAT NOW?
Well, the logic of the story suggests that after 
rescuing Marky and being returned to his family, Lurth, 
the leader of The Fire Wheels, fulfills the promise Spike 
made to the players, and will escort them to the city of 
Red Rock. Is it possible that there will be setbacks on 
the trip? Maybe some highwaymen will fall on them? If 
possible. That will depend on the GD.
Once they arrive in Red Rock, the PCs must contact 
John Hammer's client, and agree to deliver him the 
merchandise that the late Gentleman got from one of 
his odd jobs. And how will the PCs get to Mr. Hammer's 
client? Perhaps they should visit the underworld, 
and ask in gun stores, bars, nightclubs, second-hand 
stores, cyberimplant clinics, illegal gambling dens or 
even raid the database of a police station. Any of these 
activities, carried out by the GD, may lead the PCs to the 
EPILOGUE
122


WHO IS JOHN HAMMER'S CUSTOMER?
The identity of the Gentleman's client will remain 
hidden throughout this mini-campaign, with the GD 
deciding his role at the time he contacts the PCs. If 
the GD is short of ideas, he can take one of the ideas 
proposed here to give it a past and an identity.
•	A retired ex-military man, who was considered 
missing in action, during a mission in South 
America/Middle East.
• A corrupt police officer, investigated for internal 
affairs, but with his back well covered by his 
contacts in the underworld.
• The landfill owner, collector of rare and quaint 
items, and experimental cyber-implant fan.
• A paramedic and habitual drug dealer, who sells 
medical equipment on the black market.
• An executive from a pharmaceutical/technology/
weapons company, who is fighting for a promotion.
• The owner of a record company, an expert in 
public relations and with contacts among the city's 
cultural elites.
• A government agent/spy, in charge of national 
security, and specialized in covert operations.
• A smuggler of illegal material, specialized in cyber 
implants and hacking programs, who uses a laundry 
as a cover.
• The owner of a gym, who gets some extra credits 
by buying and selling illegal drugs.
• A hacker who is a member of a collective of hackers, 
who defend the free flow of information and who 
are dedicated to raiding corporate databases to 
expose (or sell) their secrets.
• The advisor of a city politician, somewhat shady and 
not very legal, in charge of achieving the objectives 
that his master entrusts to him.
• A mercenary, owner of a bar on the outskirts of 
the city, and with contacts in the police and city 
corporations.
•  The owner of a Dojo, an old and serene man, leader 
of a secret clan of ninjas and assassins.
• A scientist, head of a biochemistry department, 
belonging to a biotechnology corporation.
• A government agent, in charge of cyber security, 
who leads a group of experts in charge of fighting 
rogue AIs.
CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: EPILOGUE
• An independent reporter, with friends in the police 
and the underworld, and specialized in uncovering 
corporate and government scandals.
• A collector of late 20th century antiques, a fan of 
video games and limited AI androids.
• A retired actor, owner of a talent agency and a 
select club in the fashionable area of the city.
• The owner of a car workshop, fond of organizing 
illegal races.
• A truck driver, frequent transporter, and occasional 
smuggler.
WHAT IS MERCHANDISE?
The merchandise obtained by John Hammer will 
not actually have any importance for the plot of this 
mini-campaign. It will essentially be a McGuffin, a plot 
element that, at first glance, appears to be of capital 
importance to the script, but whose only function is to 
set the story in motion.
In any case, if the GD wants to give it some kind of plot 
value, he can shape it to his liking, or choose one of the 
following examples:
• A biological sample capsule of extraterrestrial 
origin.
•  A lost canvas from Rembrant's The Storm on the 
Sea of Galilee.
•   A film reel, with censored scenes, from an old film 
from the early 20th century.
•  The original masters of a music artist, unmixed or 
unadulterated.
•  Documents and files of prototypes of cyberimplants 
not tested on humans.
•   Strategic plans for covert operations of intelligence 
agencies.
•  A sex video of a well-known news anchor.
• Genetic samples from test subjects, resistant to 
cyberimplants.
• Secret weapon designs, from a weapons 
corporation.
•  The encrypted code of a hacker website hidden in 
the Deep Web.

CHAPTER 9: STRANGERS IN A STRANGE LAND: EPILOGUE
violent gangs. But also stimulating live music venues, 
hard-to-find cyber-implant stores and criminal bosses 
willing to offer them a well-paying job if they are willing 
to give it their all.
If the GD has another idea in mind for Red Rock, he can 
describe it however he likes.
 
In any case, here is a possible map of the city, with a few 
places of interest, marked on the map.
WHAT IS THE RED ROCK CITY LIKE?
When the PCs arrive in the city of Red Rock, the GD, 
in his description, should emphasize the immensity of 
the buildings, the wide highways, the winding streets, 
the bustling and saturated traffic. But also in the 
contrast between the ultra-technified of its wealthier 
neighborhoods with the saturation and poverty of its 
lower neighborhoods. Police patrols (private or public) 
will be common in the busiest and most prestigious 
areas, but scarce and poorly equipped in the peripheral 
and most dangerous neighborhoods. If the PCs visit the 
slums, they will easily encounter pimps, drug dealers or

APPENDIX 1: EQUIPMENT LISTS
HACKING PROGRAMS
TYPE
PRICE
FUNCTION
Backdoor
1.500 credits
Thanks to this program it is possible to increase the alert die of a node. 
Thus a 1D4 Alert Level becomes 1D6 and so on (increasing its alert die 
by one level).
Crackware
1.000 credits for each
+1 up to a maximum of +5.
Increases by +1 the Cyber skill to access a node.
Crackdown
1.000 credits for each
+2 up to a maximum of +8.
Increases by +2 the Cyber skill to access a node.
Crackdown Black
3.500 credits
Increases by +3 the Cyber skill to access a node. The PC has the option 
to increase the Cyber power by means of a 1D10 roll: If he/she rolls a 
result greater than 7, the Cyber skill will increase to +4; if the result is 
less than or equal to 7, the Cyber skill will be only +2.
Recursive Diagnosis
1.000 credits for each
+1 up to a maximum of +5.
Increases by +1 the Cyber skill to discover hidden doors and links.
Recursive Diagnosis
New Level
1.500 credits for each
+2 up to a maximum of +8.
Increases by +2 the Cyber skill to discover hidden doors and links
Exploit
2.500 credits
Take control of any level 0 node automatically, without the need to per-
form the Cyber skill roll.
Net Exploit
4.500 credits
Take control of any level 0 node automatically, without the need to per-
form the Cyber skill roll. May be used 2 times before it is used up.
Extended Net Exploit
5.500 credits
Take control of any level 1 node automatically, without the need to 
perform the Cyber skill roll.
Shield
2.500 credits
The program allows to protect the user who uses it against the first 
attack performed by the Quarantine and Black Ice countermeasures. 
Once consumed, the character will not be able to use it again during 
the raid unless he rewrites it by passing a DC=16 in Cyber. These rolls 
are considered actions within the Net and, therefore, if the test is fai-
led, the GD must roll the alert level die.
Spider
3.500 credits
Once a node is checked, the program will automatically discover all 
nodes and (non-hidden) links in the Constellation, without having to 
move from node to node.
Trojan
1.500 credits for each -1.
It allows to copy the code of an original program from the intruding 
Node and to create a disguise that confuses the security of the Net. 
Thanks to this, the DC can be reduced by -1. The maximum that can be 
reduced is -5.
Bart ´S Trojan
2.900 credits for each -2.
It allows to copy the code of an original program from the intruding 
Node and to create a disguise that confuses the security of the Net. 
Thanks to this, the DC can be reduced by -2. The maximum that can be 
reduced is -8.
Nuke
3.500 credits
The program allows attacking a Node Constellations by breaking all 
accesses, so that the hacker can go directly to the desired node to ex-
tract the information he is looking for. To achieve this, he must exceed 
the DC of the maximum level of the constellation. In addition, using 
this program triggers the alert and activates all the defensive programs 
present.
134
